2026-09-19 · An invitation to Syah

The objection I hear most is not that the idea is wrong. It is that the number is too small. A hundred and forty-seven people on a list cannot do anything.

Estonia in 1991 is the answer I keep going back to. When the occupation ended, the country had no money and infrastructure that did not work. What it had was 1.3 million people who already knew how to do things, and it built a functioning modern state out of that — largely in software, because software was what it could afford — over about two generations, while the pressure from next door never let up. I have written the numbers (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/how-the-math-works) and the posture (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/estonia-did-not-get-to-solve-it-either) up before, and nobody here is forming a nation state; the country is the yardstick and nothing more.

The part of it that bears on this post is simpler than either. Nobody waited for the money. The people who could do things were already there, and what changed was the arrangements between them.

One of the things a place has to be able to do for itself is put clothes on people.

Syah

Syah (https://www.quora.com/profile/Syah-Neal-AdoreTM), in Kansas City, Missouri. The Directory profile is at https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory/profile/fdb8086c-e241-4f51-baf9-4992892a31b8 and it carries three specializations: fit and sizing, garment construction, and textile selection and sourcing.

[Screenshot — Syah's Directory profile in the app. The name Syah, marked Community-generated profile, nominated by @farah, with a location of Kansas City, Missouri, United States. A control reads View Quora profile. Under Specializations, three tags: Fit and sizing; Garment construction; Textile selection and sourcing. Below, a panel headed Want to work together? reads: the directory shows who is in the community and what they do. Want a service or good from this person? Look for them in Foundation, where members offer and exchange help — or browse Foundation to find someone else who can.]

Those three are not three versions of one skill. Together they run from a bolt of cloth to a garment on a person who can move in it.

Textile selection and sourcing is knowing where the material comes from, what it is worth, and which of it is worth buying. Garment construction is turning it into the thing. Fit and sizing is the part most people never think about until a garment does not have it: making the thing fit a real body rather than a chart, and knowing how to alter what already exists so it does. A community that can only do the middle one of those has a sewing machine. A community that can do all three can dress itself.

Where it sits on the list

The app keeps a catalog of 657 skills — the set a working economy of about five million people needs somebody to be able to do. Apparel and fashion design is its own occupation in that catalog, filed under creative work, and making what cannot be ordered in is the job of a team the app calls Making & Repair, one of the thirteen a community has to be able to staff before it runs at all.

Clothing sits between those two and is usually forgotten by both. Planning conversations run to water, power, food and a roof, and clothing is assumed, the way it is assumed in a country with a store on every corner. It stops being assumed the first winter.

On 14 September the Directory's 147 people held 184 of the 657 skills, and 473 had nobody at all. Creative work is where the list is deepest, and it is still a list where most of the catalog is empty. Somebody who can carry a chain like that end to end on their own is not joining a queue. They are the reason the row exists.

How they came to be listed

Syah did not put themselves in the Directory. I did, after reading what they said about themselves in public, and the listing is marked community-generated and names me as the person who made it, so that nobody has to guess.

That is how most of the list was built, one person at a time. The Directory is the thing survivors ask for more than anything else: a findable list of people and what they can do. Building it by waiting for people to sign up for another app would have meant building nothing.

The invitation

Syah, the listing is under your name, and the three things on it are a chain nobody else on the list carries end to end.

Claiming it is the step that makes it yours. It takes a free account and a few minutes, and after that the entry is under your control rather than mine — you set what it says, and you can take it down.

Nothing else is required. You do not have to use the rest of the app, you do not have to post anything, and you do not have to agree with a word I have written about why any of this is happening — including the paragraph about Estonia.

If the answer is no, that is a complete answer and nothing about the listing changes. It can come down at any time, with no account involved anywhere in that. Nobody who has been asked has wanted theirs removed, and the offer has been real since the day the list went up.

This does not run one way

An invitation that only asks is not worth much to somebody already carrying a full week.

Somebody who sources cloth needs it moved, stored and paid for without going through anybody who should not be handling it, and the Directory holds more entries in transport and logistics than in any sector but creative work and health. Somebody who sews needs a ride, a room, a repair, an hour of a trade they do not have. Those are on the list too, under other people's names, and the same listing that makes them findable is what makes those findable to them.

That exchange is deliberately the first thing here rather than the last. Asking somebody for something real is also what filters out the people who will never hand anybody anything.

Estonia did not import the people who rebuilt it. They were already there, and so is Syah.

Where to find it in the app

- Directory (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#directory)
- Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#workforce)
- Foundation (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/foundation) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#foundation)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

2026-09-19 · Old links, new links

Quora has erased my accounts forty-nine times. Each erasure kills every link that ever pointed at them — in old posts, in other people's answers, in search results, in bookmarks. If you followed one of those links and landed nowhere, this page is for you.

There is one handle of mine on Quora, opened today. Everything before it is dead.

This is a living page. It is kept current, and its date moves it to the top of the feed whenever it changes. Every change is on the public record in the repository's history.

The one address that does not change

Everything I write lives here, on this blog. Every post, in full, with a timestamped history that nobody can quietly empty.

The fastest way to catch up is the feed — the short version of every post, newest first: https://chargingthefuture.com/feed

Bookmark the blog, not any platform. Platform posts are excerpts and links back here; if a platform account dies, the links from that account die and nothing else.

To reach me, save this link alongside the blog: https://sleek.bio/farah — it lists every current way to contact me, and it gets updated the same way this page does.

Quora: current

The handle: https://www.quora.com/profile/KingLaundry — opened at 7:38 in the morning on September 19, 2026, three and a half hours after the one before it was banned.

The space: https://skillseconomy.quora.com

A handle and a space, and nothing else on Quora is me. If another account claims to be, it is not. If an account or space claims to speak for the Skills Economy and is not on this page, it is not me either. Check back here, not on Quora, because this page cannot be edited by anyone but me and every change to it is on the public record.

https://www.quora.com/profile/kinghow — opened at 4:54 in the morning on September 17, 2026, forty minutes after kingcue was banned, and banned by Quora Moderation at 4:09 in the morning on September 19, 2026. Forty-seven hours. The third ban in a row to land between 3:59 and 4:14 in the morning.

Different every time, same label

Worth recording, because it is the part that settles the question.

Each of these accounts ran a different content strategy. That was deliberate. One posted at volume. One posted twice in its entire life — two short summaries with a link back here, no reposting, no back catalog. Others sat between. Exactly one of them ever received a warning, and I changed how I posted after it.

Some did not last long enough to have a strategy at all. The shortest was kingplaza-1, banned inside the minute it was opened. After it, kingcard at five minutes and twelve seconds, kingdone nine seconds longer, and the two either side of kingdone that same morning at six minutes and fifty-one. The handle kingsroad was opened to replace the account banned the day before, and was banned the next day. Then kingsyou replaced kingsroad, and was banned the next day. Whatever is deciding this is not reading the posts, because in those two cases there was almost nothing to read.

They ended the same way regardless, and the reason given is the same boilerplate every time: financial gain and spam. Neither is true.

I have the notices for seven of the August bans — August 19, August 21, August 22, August 23, August 27, August 28 and August 30. The sentence naming the reason is word for word identical in all seven: the account was linked to multiple violations of Quora's spam policies. Seven different accounts, seven different amounts of posting, one sentence.

Financial gain first, because there is exactly one thing sitting behind that claim. One account posted that the app cost a dollar a month. That account was banned.

A dollar a month did not cover the hosting bill, never mind break even — I was donating plasma to keep the thing online, which is on this blog too. The price lasted a few months and then I made the app permanently free, which it has been ever since.

So that is the entire basis: one post, one price, one account, and a price that lost money every month it existed. Every account after it mentioned no cost at all, because there is no cost. Those were banned too, for financial gain.

And if writing about something you built counted as financial gain, the rule would take out a large share of the accounts on the platform. Quora runs ads between the posts and has a paid tier that writers earn from — monetizing there is not merely permitted, it is built into the place. Charging people to read me on Quora would be fine. Giving it away is not.

Spam is easier to answer. Two posts across an account's entire life is not spam under any definition anyone uses, and that account was banned like the rest.

So the label does not describe what happened. And when you change the variable and the outcome does not move, the variable was not the cause. There is no version of my posting that survives, and no adjustment left to make. That is worth knowing plainly rather than spending another account finding out.

Nothing those accounts posted is lost. The posts are being restored to the archive section of this blog, each marked with the account it came from, the date it was originally posted, and a link to the original question where one still exists.

Elsewhere: dead

https://chargingthefuture.discourse.group — the Discourse blog, closed December 23, 2025. Every post was exported before it closed and lives in the archive here.

The old GitHub wiki — an inactive mirror. It no longer receives updates; this blog is the canonical copy.

The rule behind all of it

Nothing is written on a platform first anymore. Every post starts here, and platforms only ever get a short version and a link back. So the next time an account is erased — and I assume there will be a next time — the cost is one entry moving from the current list to the dead list on this page. That is all.

Since this page first went up, that has happened forty-seven times. The list above is current, and KingLaundry is the entry waiting to move onto it.

2026-09-19 · They cannot be ignored

Some time ago I read a post on Quora by a self-identified Targeted Individual. I did not save the link, so this is my memory of it rather than their words: they wrote that you used to know who these people were — it was the feds — and now nobody has any idea who is doing it.

What stayed with me was not the theory. It was the shape of the situation. Here was somebody who had known about targeting, had known somebody else was being targeted, and had — I will not say decided — effectively ignored it. And then it came for them, and now they are in a panic, asking who these people are.

There is a well-known line about this shape. Martin Niemöller, a German pastor, said it after the Second World War, and the wording varies from telling to telling: first they came for one group, and I said nothing, because I was not one of them. Then they came for another. Then they came for me, and there was no one left to speak. I learned it at school when we studied the war, and it is the same thing.

Thirty-four years of ignoring them

I am going to use myself as the example, on purpose. I do not want this to read as though ignoring is a failing that the person on Quora has and I do not.

I ignored them from age five to thirty-nine. That is a long stretch, and none of it changed anything.

During those years people would say things to me. I am proud of you for not reacting to them. I am proud of you for not becoming a terrorist. I am proud of you for not killing any of them. The people who say that are Specterati operators themselves — the Dictionary (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/Dictionary) carries the vocabulary — and I find it repulsive. They say it and I let it pass. In my opinion it is the wrong stance, and there is nothing to be had from arguing with it.

The first time one of them punched me in the face came after exactly that: years of no reaction, at the end of one of their cycles of extreme harassment. Their methods escalated to violence because the quieter ones were not producing the result. When you are a child the harassment runs around the clock too, but the impact is smaller and it is done less openly, because done to a child it starts to look like what it is, and the deniability goes. Doing it to an adult is still perversion.

So I was not reacting, they hit me anyway, and after that the compliments about my restraint started. Thirty-four years. It meant nothing to them. They went on to twenty-four hours a day regardless.

Why ignoring is the natural response, and why I did it

It would be easy to read this as criticism of the person on Quora, or of anybody who has turned a blind eye. It is not. Ignoring is what a person does, and I did it for most of my life for reasons I still hold.

One: do your best not to become one of them. Do not internalize it, and do not turn into the thing that is being done to you. That is my personal strategy, and a lot of the ignoring was that.

Two: I have no desire to take part in a global human trafficking network. I am completely fine being rejected by their space. Ignoring them was a way of declining to enter it.

Both reasons still stand. What does not stand is the idea that ignoring is enough.

What the person on Quora proves

Somebody knew about targeting, ignored it, and became a target anyway. That settles two things.

First, it finishes the lie about deserving it. The line that a Targeted Individual did something to merit this is said by agents of crime, and an agent of crime saying somebody should be targeted carries the weight of a car salesman saying the stranger who just walked in deserves a brand-new car, whether or not they can afford one. Rebecca L. Norrington — an acquaintance, never met, no relationship to speak of — wrote to me to say that I deserve to be trafficked and that she enjoys it. Of course she advocates the abuse of other people. She is one of the people doing it.

Second: they cannot be ignored. You do not get the choice. Not as a matter of principle — as a matter of what happens.

What "cannot be ignored" means

Think of how people deal with a parasite or a disease. You try to stay away from it. But staying away is done one of two ways: coexistence or elimination. A vaccine, a cure, a way of surviving the encounter with the damage limited — or, sometimes, wiping the thing out. People generally choose coexistence. So do I.

I want to be exact here, because it is easy to misread. I am not advocating that Targeted Individuals go out and cause harm. And I am not saying that a Targeted Individual who harms a criminal in self-defense is a terrorist. I want that very clear. They ran me over with a car. They run up to me and punch me in the face. Self-defense is not what I take issue with; I believe in it. Every Targeted Individual's threat model is different, and some of them have to account for the fact that the operators already escalated to violence because everything else was not working.

Coexistence over conspiracy, then. I could spend my life on which organization it is. I would rather build a way to live alongside a threat that is not going away.

There is also a narrower thing I do not mean. When I say you cannot ignore them, I am not talking about the small disruptions in a day. We are outnumbered, it runs around the clock, it is in the digital space and the physical one, and a person cannot answer every one of them and still have a life. Those you recognize and let go. What cannot be ignored is the lifelong thing.

Some people had COVID with no symptoms. They got to ignore it. It still had to be dealt with, because it was landing on everybody else, and the same is true here. Human traffickers are no different from terrorists in the one sense that matters: a threat to life has to be planned against, and it cannot be waved away.

What the Skills Economy is for

It is my belief that as Targeted Individuals we do not, one at a time, have the tools to not ignore them properly. I did not. So I built the thing that gives those tools to all of us at once.

My threat model is physical safety. For me, living among like-minded people, with a gate, is coexistence — it is my way of not ignoring them. A lot of people hear a closed community and think isolation. It is not isolation. It is survival, and it is living with people who understand. I do not believe in integrating with their economy. It is counterintuitive, because a barrier looks like looking away. It is the opposite. It is full recognition of the problem, followed by something done about it.

The other threat is destitution. They take your employment. They have decided your labor is worth nothing to them, and the harsh reality is that your skills are extremely valuable. The Skills Economy is how that threat gets mitigated: you trade with other people who need what you can do and can do what you need.

Two threats, two answers, and neither of them is ignoring anything.

For the people who are doing well

I want to say something to Targeted Individuals who are doing well — in quotation marks, because that is whatever it means to you.

Stay connected to other Targeted Individuals. I have watched what happens otherwise. One person did not realize how badly they were outnumbered, because although operators knew they were targeted, not many of them bothered to do it to their face. A lot can be done from a distance. Disrupting somebody's employment does not require ever being in the same room as them. Norrington had to write to me to tell me what she had been doing, because I had no other way of knowing. So this person, seeing fewer people, concluded there were fewer people, and that their targeting was not that bad.

In one way that is good. Less stress, less anxiety. I deliberately do not point out everything, because it is not healthy to. The downside is that a person in that position can fall into destitution, and once they are there, there is nobody around to help. You do not know when a series of events will arrive at the extreme one. The person on Quora is the example: it fell to that point, and now they are asking how to recover. You cannot recover from being a Targeted Individual. Once your name is on the list it is never taken off.

So even if you are doing well, the Skills Economy thriving and available is still a benefit to you. Keep at least one leg in the door.

And the next generation

I have written before about children (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/your-children-can-have-an-option). At five years old I had no options. I could not run away — I knew nothing about the world, I would have been kidnapped, I would have been trafficked in the way most people picture the word. So I was stuck, with family and with the community, and there was no leaving it as a minor without severe consequences to my own safety.

The Skills Economy succeeding is what makes an actual option exist for the next child in that position.

They cannot be ignored. Not because I am telling you not to. Because it does not work, and I have thirty-four years that say so.

2026-09-19 · What can be stopped

A question I opened as a Quora answer draft on August 6, 2025, and never wrote:

Is it possible for targeted individuals to stop being harassed by gang stalkers? If so, what methods have been successful in stopping the harassment?

The question is at https://www.quora.com/Is-it-possible-for-targeted-individuals-to-stop-being-harassed-by-gang-stalkers-If-so-what-methods-have-been-successful-in-stopping-the-harassment — go and read it where it stands. Nobody asked it. It was added on October 11, 2024 by Quora Prompt Generator, a bot Quora runs to seed questions, which I did not notice until after I had written this. I am keeping it because it is, almost word for word, the question survivors keep asking in their own words: the list of saved Quora questions this blog works from holds a dozen versions of it. The vocabulary I use for the people running this is in the Dictionary (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/Dictionary); the question keeps the words it was asked in.

[Screenshot — The question on Quora, captured September 19, 2026. A line above it reads: Question added by Quora Prompt Generator. The question reads: Is it possible for targeted individuals to stop being harassed by gang stalkers? If so, what methods have been successful in stopping the harassment? Below it: #3978753715, Thank, Report, October 11, 2024 at 3:43:47 AM.]

I asked this one too, in different words, for most of my life. So here is the answer I would have wanted, which is not the one I was hoping for.

No method I know of has stopped it

I have been targeted since I was five and I am forty-one. I have not found a way to end it, and I have not met a survivor who has. Ignoring it for thirty-four years changed nothing (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/they-cannot-be-ignored); the first time one of them punched me in the face came after years of no reaction at all. There is no place in the United States I could move to that is free of it.

Two things about the accounts of it stopping.

The first is that a quiet stretch is not the same as an ending. I was asked once whether doing better meant fewer operators were around. No. They change how they work. Laying low is regrouping, so that the guard comes down before the overt part resumes. A person who counts fewer of them in their face and concludes there are fewer has counted the wrong thing.

The second is who tells survivors it will stop. Specterati operators do. They will lean into any story a person accepts: that this is a watch list and it lifts, that it is spiritual and ends at enlightenment, that it is a test and a pass exists. I was told I am a truth teller and they are the Illuminati. The goal post moves every time, because the story is a method and not a promise. Somebody who reaches the finish line they were given finds another one drawn past it.

Could they stop? In theory. But a person's exploitation is worth something to the people doing it, and torturing one person is also how the rest of the operatives are kept in line. Nothing about that arithmetic changes because the person asked nicely, or moved, or went quiet.

What can be stopped

So the honest answer to the first half is no. The second half has a better answer than it looks, once the question is asked about the right thing.

What they are after is not the harassment for its own sake. It is what the harassment costs: your account of your own life, your day, your work, your housing, and who you have to go through to get any of those. Every one of those costs can be cut, and cutting them is the only thing I have watched work.

Your record. A dated log you own means your account of your life stops depending on memory under stress. ClickLog is that: date, a location if you choose one, fifteen seconds. It does not stop an incident. It stops the incident from also taking your credibility with it, and it means you can put the noticing down instead of carrying it all day.

Your day. The theatre is built to be studied, and every hour spent mapping it is an hour they took. I spent nine months on that and the turn is dated on the record (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/from-them-to-me). What replaces it is something that compounds, that is still there next month whatever they did this week.

Your footage. For years operators started fights with me in public so that new recruits could be shown a person scuffling in the street. Not supplying that is not composure. It is declining to make the picture, and it is the one thing in a staged incident that is yours to decide.

Your dependence. This is the largest one and the reason this project is an economy rather than an awareness campaign. Being watched is not what gives someone power over you. Needing something from them is. (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/estonia-did-not-get-to-solve-it-either) Who fixes your car, who you work for, who rents you a room, who drives you: each of those is a choice about whose hands your week runs through, and each one moved is leverage taken off the table. Estonia did not get to choose its neighbor. It chose its arrangements, and thirty years of that is the difference between surviving and doing well.

Coexistence, and why it is not giving up

I hold to coexistence over conspiracy (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/how-are-you-doing). I could spend the rest of my life on which organization it is. My position is that it is a great many ordinary people in a distributed network of organized crime, so there is no single door to knock on and no petition that closes it.

That is not resignation. It is the same posture a person takes toward any hazard that is not going to leave: you stop organizing your life around its ending and start organizing it around your own footing. The communities of 1903 did not get an ending either (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/how-few-people-it-takes), and they built trade schools and businesses anyway, one generation at a time. A hundred survivors trading with each other is not a support group with a spreadsheet. It is the part of the answer that was ever in reach.

And the part I would say to anybody who has been told it stopped: stay connected anyway. Once a name is on the list it is never taken off, and the quiet years are when people drift into destitution with nobody around. Keep one leg in the door.

What none of this promises

It does not promise the harassment ends. I do not believe that, and I am not going to write it because it would sell.

It promises something smaller and real. The harassment stops being the only checkable thing about you. It stops setting your schedule. It stops deciding who you rent from and who you work for. And the people who matter, the ones who turn up with a ride or a room, are not deciding anything about you based on it.

The targeting is real. It is not the most interesting fact about you.

Where to find it in the app

- ClickLog (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/click-log) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#click-log)
- Directory (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#directory)
- Foundation (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/foundation) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#foundation)
- Trust (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/trust) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#trust)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

2026-09-18 · An invitation to Espada

The app keeps a list of thirteen jobs that a community has to be able to fill before it can run at all.

Water and sanitation. Building and infrastructure. Food. Health. Safety. Making and repair. Education. Operations. Six more. It is not a wish list — it is the set of things where, if nobody can do it, the lights are on and the place still does not work.

No one person fills thirteen jobs and nobody is asked to. What a list is for is seeing which of them are thin.

Espada

Espada (https://www.quora.com/profile/ESPADA-18), in Detroit, Michigan. The Directory profile is at https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory/profile/42506e2d-d81a-4455-b3fd-bd76984ecdf0 and it carries three specializations — plumber, CCTV, small appliance repair.

[Screenshot — Espada's Directory profile in the app. The name Espada, marked Community-generated profile, nominated by @farah, with a location of Detroit, Michigan, United States. A control reads View Quora profile. Under About: Small engine repair, installing CCTV, appliance repair, handyman, plumber's apprentice. Under Specializations, three tags: Plumber; CCTV; Small Appliance Repair. Below, a panel headed Want to work together? reads: the directory shows who is in the community and what they do. Want a service or good from this person? Look for them in Foundation, where members offer and exchange help — or browse Foundation to find someone else who can.]

Their own description of the work is longer than the tags: small engine repair, installing CCTV, appliance repair, handyman, plumber's apprentice.

Apprentice is the word they used, and it is the one worth keeping. The tag on the listing reads plumber, which is the label the catalog had to hand. Somebody learning a trade is not the same as somebody who has finished learning it, and saying otherwise would be putting a claim in their mouth.

Those five kinds of work land on five different jobs out of the thirteen.

Plumbing is its own job on that list and also part of another. The app describes the Water & Sanitation team as drinking water and wastewater once the taps exist — sources and wells, treatment, testing, distribution, repairs — and separates it from Build & Infrastructure, which installs the thing in the first place. One of those puts the pipe in. The other is why the water coming out of it is safe a year later.

Small engine repair and appliance repair are Making & Repair, which the app describes as keeping equipment running past the point where a supplier would replace it. That sentence was written before anybody read Espada's listing, and it is a description of what they already do.

Handyman work is Operations & Maintenance — the maintenance rosters, the shared things that have to keep working, the jobs nobody has a title for.

CCTV sits in Safety & Security, which is the gate, the perimeter, and knowing what actually happened rather than arguing about it afterward.

What the list actually holds

The skills catalog carries 657 entries — the set of things a working economy of about five million people needs somebody to be able to do. On 14 September the Directory's 147 people held 184 of them.

473 have nobody at all.

Some of the gaps are wide in a way that is easy to miss because the number is small:

- Water and sanitation: 3 skills held, out of 19 in the catalog.
- Energy and utilities: 3 held, out of 20.
- Public safety and justice: 1 held, out of 15.
- Manufacturing and industry, which is where making and mending sits: 11 held, out of 28.

Those are not competitive fields where somebody would be joining a queue. They are close to empty.

The only part of the catalog the Directory has finished is retail and services — all fourteen of its skills held. Everything else has room, and the rooms this listing opens onto are among the emptiest.

Which is where the word apprentice stops being a qualifier. Water and sanitation is three skills out of nineteen, held across a list of 147 people. A trade that thin is not short of masters — it is short of anybody at all, and the person currently learning it is the one who ends up holding it. On a finished list somebody early in a trade is behind. On this one they are the front of it.

Why this is an invitation rather than an announcement

Espada did not sign up. Most people on the Directory did not.

The list was built by reading what people said about themselves in public and writing it down under their name, one at a time, which is why an entry is marked community-generated until its owner claims it — the listing says so on its face, and names me as the person who put it there. Saying that plainly is the honest part, and it answers the question anybody would be asking by now.

Claiming the listing is what makes it theirs rather than mine. It takes a free account and a few minutes, and after that they set what it says and can take it down.

Nothing else is required. They do not have to use the rest of the app, do not have to post anything, and do not have to agree with a word I have written about why any of this is happening.

If the answer is no, that is a complete answer and nothing about the listing changes. It can come down at any time, with no account involved anywhere in that. Nobody who has been asked has wanted theirs removed, and the offer has been real since the day the list went up.

This does not run one way

An invitation that only asks is not worth much to somebody already carrying a full week.

Somebody who fixes what other people rely on needs things too, and usually needs them from a stranger. A ride that does not go through somebody who should not be driving. A room. An hour of a trade they do not have. Those are on the list as well, under other people's names, and the same listing that makes them findable is what makes those findable to them.

That exchange is deliberately the first thing here rather than the last. Asking somebody for something real is also what filters out the people who will never hand anybody anything.

Where to find it in the app

- Directory (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#directory)
- Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#workforce)
- SkillsHunt (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skills-hunt) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#skills-hunt)
- Foundation (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/foundation) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#foundation)

2026-09-18 · How are you doing?

There is a thing I want to name, because once you can see it you cannot stop seeing it, and it explains a category of interaction that otherwise reads as confusing or even friendly.

Performed kindness. Kind words, said in a way that is not kind. The words are the cover and the delivery is the content.

What it looks like

Somebody holds a door for me and then says something as I go through it. The door was never the point — walking close enough to say the thing was. If I had opened it myself there would have been no opening to speak into.

Somebody walks past a woman and tells her she should smile more. Men stand around with the same face on and are never told. It is said to women, it is framed as encouragement, and it is an instruction from a stranger about what her face should be doing.

In a public laundry room, somebody took issue with me for moving a stranger's washing out of a machine and not putting it in a dryer. Every washer was full. Nobody is supposed to leave clothes sitting in a public laundry for an extended period of time, and when they do, the clothes get moved — that is how the room works, and moving them is not taking them. I did not put the load in a dryer on purpose. You do not run a dryer on somebody else's clothes unless you know them and know how they do their washing. Heat shrinks things. Some of it should be on a lower setting and some of it should not go in at all.

So the complaint was that I had failed to do the thing that would have wrecked a stranger's clothes.

That is the shape of all of it. Saying something costs nothing. It buys the appearance of being a good person, by their definition, and it usually comes with somebody watching.

And then the question

Which brings me to the one in the title.

Their raw material is your pain. Not your work, not your time, not anything you produce. What they are collecting is how badly you are doing, and they collect it by asking.

So when the question arrives from that direction, it is not concern. It is a meter reading. A bad answer is a good day for them.

I want to be exact here, because this is the part that gets misread. This is not about Targeted Individuals asking each other how they are doing. That is an ordinary human thing to say and most people mean it. No survivor has ever asked me it in bad faith, and if you have asked me it, nothing in this post is aimed at you.

What I am describing is one direction only, and what I am announcing is my own choice, for myself.

Why the raw material changed

It is worth being precise about what is being taken, because the answer is not what most people assume.

The transatlantic slave trade wanted labor. That was the entire economic mechanism: take the work for free, drive the cost of goods down, sell them, and never pay a wage. Horrific, and legible — the value being extracted was work, and the bondage existed to keep the work coming.

That is not a comparison and I am not making one. I am pointing at one difference, and only one.

What is done to Targeted Individuals now runs the opposite way. Your work is the first thing destroyed. References dry up, the role is restructured, the contract is not renewed, the business is driven to zero. If your labor were the valuable thing, nobody would spend years making sure you cannot do any.

You are pushed out of the economy and you still have every skill you had. The skills were never what was wanted.

So the raw material changed. It is the suffering itself — and an economy built on that has to keep producing it, which is why the harassment is continuous rather than aimed at anything in particular.

Slavery at least binds a person to labor, because the labor is the product. Here there is no labor anybody wants. That is a structural fact about the arrangement, not a ranking of whose suffering was worse, and I am not interested in that argument.

Why I am not Dr. King

They tell me I act like Martin Luther King Jr.

I do not, and it is not modesty to say so. I have accomplished nothing next to what he did, and I am not on the same mission. When they say it, they are not describing me. They are describing what they want, or what they have already been doing to me since I was five.

But there is a real difference underneath, and it is worth writing down, because it is a difference about strategy rather than about him.

His last campaign was economic. Wages, jobs, a union for sanitation workers in Memphis, a Poor People's Campaign built on economic demands. The ask was to be let in, on fair terms, to a system that was refusing. He was killed in the middle of that work.

My ask is not to be let in.

I am not asking to be integrated into an arrangement run by people who have spent my whole life taking things from me. I do not want a seat at that table on fair terms. I want a different table, built by people who are not doing this, where what I can do is worth something to somebody who is also not doing this.

Coexistence over conspiracy.

That is a contrast, not a criticism. He was answering the question in front of him and the answer he reached was the right one for it. The question in front of me is different: the arrangement I am excluded from does not want my labor and will not trade for it, so being let in buys nothing.

Coexistence over conspiracy

The other half of that phrase is about where the conversation goes.

I could spend the rest of my life on which organization it is, which agency, which person, who started it. I have written about who and why, and I will again — but only where it connects to what to do next. On its own it is a hole you can stand in for a decade.

My honest position is that it is not one organization. It is a great many ordinary people inside a distributed network of organized crime, and survivors are outnumbered by something like three hundred to one at the lowest estimate I can defend. There is no single door to knock on.

So I would rather talk about the other thing. Building somewhere to live and work among people who are not part of it. Not integration. Not exposure as a strategy. Coexistence — with each other.

What I ask instead

I am not going to ask you how you are doing.

I already know. I am living the same week you are, and the answer costs you something to say out loud for no return.

I am going to ask what you need.

It is a question with an answer that somebody can act on. A ride. A room. A repair. An hour of a trade you do not have. If the answer is a thing, the thing can be arranged, and afterwards you have it.

That is not me being impersonal, and it is not a transaction standing in for care. It is the opposite of what the other direction does. They ask so they can take the answer. I ask so I can do something about it.

It also settles a question you are entitled to have about me. A Specterati operator is in the business of taking everything from you — anything you want and need, they will work to remove. Somebody who asks what you need and then arranges it is doing the reverse, in public, where it can be checked.

So why delete the account

Here is a question I keep turning over.

If none of this worked, why does Quora keep erasing me?

I do not promote self-harm. I do not promote harm to anybody. Nothing I have written asks anyone to confront, expose, or retaliate against a single person. It is a peaceful thing with an economic answer at the end of it, and the accounts die anyway, forty-eight times now, with the same boilerplate reason attached every time.

You do not spend that much effort on something that is not reaching anybody.

Being copied, and what that tells you

The other signal is on the platform itself.

I now see Targeted Individuals on Quora — some real, some not — reflecting parts of the Skills Economy back. The idea that there is a way to live other than in misery, that survivors could be trading with each other, that a list of who can do what is worth having.

Imitation is flattery and I mean that plainly. It is good that the alternative is being seen. I am not in competition with anybody, and I want to be very clear about that: more communities is the goal, not fewer. A distributed peer-to-peer network is exactly what this is supposed to be. If five of these exist and none of them is mine, the thing I wanted still happened.

But there is a difference in the copies that matters, and it is not a small one.

Most of them are open to anybody. Which means real survivors in those spaces are interacting with operators without knowing it, because nothing filters. A space with no filter hands them a seat at the table, and a seat at the table is the thing they were denied here.

I do not give them one. That is why the raw material stops being available, and I suspect that is closer to the real reason an account gets erased than anything in the posts.

Where the tipping point is

So here is the question I do not have a clean answer to, and I would rather put it to you than pretend.

What happens at ninety to ten? A community that is ninety percent real survivors and ten percent operators — does it still function?

What about fifty-fifty? At what ratio does a group stop being able to make any progress at all, because everything said in it is being carried straight back?

I do not know the number. What I know is that there is one, and that every community has to find it before it finds them.

If you are running one of these spaces, you are going to have to work out two thresholds. The first is the platform's: how much reach you can have before the host decides to end you, which is a line Quora has drawn on me forty-eight times. The second is internal: how many operators can be inside before what you are building stops working.

For the Skills Economy my answer is one. One is too many.

The way that is done is not by interrogating anybody or accusing anybody. It is that the whole thing is built around exchange. You ask for something real, and it either arrives or it does not. Their raw material is your suffering, not output, so requiring output filters them out early without anybody having to make an accusation they cannot prove.

Why I do this at all

People thank me for working on it, and the honest answer is that I get a mutual benefit. There is no charge for any of this and it is not revenue for me.

The real reason is simpler. For a long time the only place to find other Targeted Individuals in a virtual space was Quora, and it took me two years of looking to find people who could actually help me and who I could actually help back. Two years, by hand, filtering out operators pretending to be survivors, and survivors who did not want to help, and survivors who wanted to and could not.

Nobody survives this alone. That is not a slogan, it is arithmetic — you need somebody to exchange with, and finding them should not cost the next person two years.

Buying an email list

There is a version of all this that grows faster and I am not going to build it.

The fast version is performed kindness at scale. Content written to be clicked. Gestures made where people can see them. Numbers that go up because the number going up is the product.

It is the same instinct as buying an email list. You end up with a bigger figure and nothing underneath it, and everybody involved can tell.

Somebody told me recently that the app is dead because not many people are on it. There is no gimmick in it to make that number move, which is deliberate. The work happens slowly, in the actual arrangements between two people, and most of it never shows up as a figure anywhere.

I would rather have the small true number.

So

I am not going to ask you how you are doing.

I am going to ask what you need, and then I am going to see whether the Skills Economy can get it to you. That is the offer, it does not expire, and it is not collecting anything from you on the way through.

Where to find it in the app

- Directory (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#directory)
- Foundation (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/foundation) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#foundation)
- Unlock (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/plugin/unlock) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#unlock)
- Fireside (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/fireside) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#fireside)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

Full post: https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/how-are-you-doing

==============================================================================
2026-09-18 · How to run a one percent session

In the last post (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/my-turn) I said what I can offer, beyond building the app: sitting with Targeted Individuals over their own figures.

There is an obvious problem with that offer. There is one of me.

So here is the whole method, written down. Run it on yourself. Run it for somebody else. You do not need me in the room and you do not need anything from the app to do it — the app makes one step faster, and that is all.

What it is

Eight questions, in order. They turn a list of what you can do into a figure, and then turn the figure into the first thing to do about it.

It takes about an hour. Most of that is the last three questions; the first five go quicker than you expect.

Before you start

If you are doing this for somebody else, read their listing or ask them what they can do, and nothing else. Do not go researching a person before you sit down with them. Whatever turns up shapes how you hear them and they did not offer it.

If you are doing it alone, write your answers down as you go. This does not work in your head. The figure in particular has to be seen.

Two things to say first, if it is for somebody else

Both before anything else, because both change what a person feels able to say.

Nothing about their standing changes. Not because of what they say, not because they end it early, not because they say no to the whole thing. If you have any power over them at all — you run the group, you made the list, you know people — say this out loud rather than assuming it is understood.

Notes are not publication. If you are writing anything down, say so, and say that writing it down is not permission to repeat it. That is a separate question, asked separately, later, and never in the same conversation as arranging help.

The eight

1. What do you need?

Not how are you doing. I have written about why (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/how-are-you-doing) — the short version is that one of those questions has an answer somebody can act on and the other collects something.

Take the answer as given. Do not fix it yet.

2. Is the list of what you can do right?

Read their skills back to them, or read your own. What is missing, what is wrong, what would come off.

Most people have never checked. If the list was built for somebody rather than by them, this is often the first time anybody has read it to them, and the corrections are usually substantial.

3. What would you charge one person, once?

Their rate. Not a market rate they read somewhere, not what they think they are allowed to ask, and not an aspiration.

What would they actually ask a real person for the thing they actually do. If they have never charged for it, ask what would make it worth the trouble.

This question stalls people. Let it. The stall is information.

4. Run the arithmetic in front of them

One percent of five million survivors is fifty thousand people. Multiply.

Then say what the number is and what it is not. It is a target with their own rate underneath it. It is not a forecast, it is not income, and nobody is owed it.

The comparison worth giving: the app's Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) screen splits a whole economy evenly and lands near $142,500 a person. No real outcome is an even split. Doing well comes out of three parts — talent, luck, and how much work goes in — and none of the three is evenly distributed. The average has all of it averaged away, which is why nobody lands on it.

If they have an account, the What's your 1%? tab does this multiplication for them off their listing. If they do not, a calculator does the same job.

5. Who is the first one?

Not the fifty thousandth. The first person who could pay that rate for that thing.

A name if they have one. A kind of person if they do not.

6. What stands between you and that one?

Let it be concrete. The answer is almost always material: no transport, no address, no tools, no room to work in, no way to be found by anybody looking.

Write the specific obstacle, not the category. "Transport" is a category. "No way to get to a client across town on a Tuesday morning" is an obstacle, and an obstacle has things you can do about it.

7. What can be arranged about that specific thing?

This is where a tool is a tool. The Directory (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory) makes somebody findable. Foundation (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/foundation) is where an exchange gets offered or asked for. SkillsHunt (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skills-hunt) finds a person with the trade that is missing. SkillUp (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skill-up) teaches one or learns one.

If none of that touches the obstacle, say so plainly. Reaching for a tool that does not fit is worse than admitting the gap, and the gap is worth knowing.

8. One next action, with a name against it

One. Not five.

Who does it, and roughly when. A session that ends in five good ideas ends in nothing, and everybody involved knows it by the following week.

Afterwards

Write it down the same day if you are helping somebody. Memory rewrites a conversation inside twenty-four hours and the figures go first.

Then do the thing you said you would do. The conversation was not the work.

What this is not

Not therapy. It is not a place to take a detailed account of what has been done to somebody. If that is what is needed, it is a different conversation with a different person, and this one should say so rather than absorbing it.

Not a sales call. Nobody has to join anything, use the app, or agree with a word I have written for the hour to be worth their time. Every question above works with a pen and a calculator.

Not a promise. The figure is arithmetic on an estimate. Saying that is part of the session, not a disclaimer at the end of it.

Why it is written down rather than offered

Because one person cannot run five hundred thousand sessions, and a method that only exists inside somebody's head reaches exactly as far as that person's calendar.

It is also the honest answer to anybody who suspects this is a funnel. Here is the entire thing.

Where to find it in the app

- Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#workforce)
- Directory (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#directory)
- Foundation (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/foundation) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#foundation)
- SkillsHunt (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skills-hunt) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#skills-hunt)
- SkillUp (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skill-up) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#skill-up)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

2026-09-18 · My turn

Every week I ask somebody to look at what they can do and consider offering it here, as work or as a hobby, on their own terms.

It is unreasonable to keep asking that without answering it myself. So this post is my turn.

First, an artist

Before anything else, I am an artist. That is not a recent decision and it is not a job title.

I remember the first drawing I gave away. It was for a school project. Somebody saw it and asked to keep it — not politely, but with real enthusiasm, the way you ask for something you actually want.

I already knew I could draw. What I did not know until that moment was what it feels like when something you made lands on another person. That part felt like magic and it has never stopped feeling that way.

This is not about selling art. Selling art is fine and plenty of people should. It is that I drew for myself, I enjoyed every minute of it, and it made somebody else happy at the same time. Those three things arriving together is the thing I have been chasing ever since, and the artist in me has shown up in everything I have built since, including this app.

Then 2023

It has been going since I was five. For most of that time it came in cycles — a stretch of it, then quiet, then a stretch of it again.

In 2023 it went continuous. It also got bigger. It used to be a selective few; more exposure brought in more of the groups, and the head count went up with it.

That changes the question a person asks about their own future. Not what do I want to be, which is a question for somebody with a straight road in front of them. More like: given this, what am I now.

Can I say sociologist

I majored in sociology. For years I did not feel I could claim the word.

That hesitation was not modesty. It is what happens when you learn to describe yourself in the vocabulary of employment: a title is something an employer confers, so without one you are nothing in particular. The Skills Economy exists partly because that vocabulary is wrong. A person is a plumber because they can do plumbing. The title is a separate object and it belongs to somebody else.

By my own standard, then, the honest answer is yes.

Not because of the degree. Because of what the last two years have actually consisted of. Naming and maintaining a list of methods so incidents can be counted. Building a taxonomy of the work a community needs, and one of the ways it gets taken apart. Collecting incidents with tags so a pattern can be seen that no single person could see alone. Running a survey about account removals, and a separate observational census of the accounts still standing, because self-report and observation answer different questions and have to be read together. Refusing to gate that survey behind approval, because gating it would have sampled only the people already reached.

That last one is the giveaway. Worrying about who your sample excludes before you collect it is not a thing a person does by accident.

So: an artist who does sociology. I am not employed as one and I do not publish in journals, and I am not going to pretend those are the same as what I do. But I study how a group of people organizes itself and what that does to the people inside it, I do it with method, and the record of it is public and dated.

I will name the limits, because leaving them out would undo the claim. I am inside what I am studying, which is a real method with a real name and a known cost: there are things your own position hides from you. My sample comes mostly from one platform. And I have no comparison group, which is the largest gap in all of it and the hardest to fix.

What I set down

Something else changed and it belongs in an honest accounting.

I used to place myself firmly as pro-Black. I do not any more.

Everybody already knew that space had its own operation running through it. What changed it for me was seeing how many Black people are involved in trafficking, inside a psyop shaped the same way. Once I had seen that from the inside, the category stopped describing anything I could organize around.

I do not regret the years, and not as a consolation. Those years were spent reading, watching, and working out how a thing is structured and who benefits — and that is the same work I do now. It made the Skills Economy possible to design, and it has been what kept me functional inside being trafficked. The training was real even though the conclusion did not survive.

What I can offer

Which brings me to the part I am actually announcing.

Beyond building the app and running the logistics inside it, what I think I can offer is this: helping other Targeted Individuals reach their one percent.

There is a tab in Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) called What's your 1%? It reads the skills already on your Directory listing, you set a rate you would actually charge, and it shows you what serving one percent of five million survivors — fifty thousand people — comes to in a year.

The main Workforce screen splits the whole economy evenly and gets about $142,500 a person. No real outcome is an even split. However you define doing well, at work or at the thing you do for love, it comes out of three parts: talent, luck, and how much work you put in. The even split has all three of those averaged away.

So the figure on that tab is not a prediction. It is a target with your own arithmetic under it, and the question it leaves you with is the interesting one. What would you actually have to do to get there? What is the first thing? Who would you need?

That is what I want to help people work on. Not signing up — the app is a logistics tool, and no logistics tool has ever been the reason anybody's life changed. The thing underneath it is a person deciding there is a version of this where they are doing their work again, and then having somebody to work it through with.

What I am going to do

I am going to start reaching out to Targeted Individuals about more than joining something.

The invitation posts will carry on, because being findable matters and most people on that list still do not know they are on it. But the next thing I want to be doing is sitting with people over their own figures. What you can do, what you would charge, what fifty thousand of them would mean, and what stands between you and the first one.

I built the map. Walking it with people is the part I can offer, and it is the part I would enjoy — which, going back to the drawing, has always been how I can tell a thing is mine to do.

Where to find it in the app

- Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#workforce)
- Directory (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#directory)
- Foundation (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/foundation) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#foundation)
- ClickLog (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/click-log) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#click-log)
- SkillsHunt (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skills-hunt) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#skills-hunt)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

2026-09-17 · An invitation to Christy

A community that can stand up on its own needs two things to be true at the same time.

Somebody has to keep people well. And somebody has to build the things people stand inside.

Most lists have those on opposite sides of a room. There is one person on the Directory who is listed for both.

Christy

Christy (https://www.quora.com/profile/None-Ya-970), in the United States. Her Directory profile is at https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory/profile/173cb388-7df9-4079-a048-4de2d64b2bf4 and it carries eight skills across two trades.

[Screenshot — Christy's Directory profile in the app. The name Christy, with the line Just about anything. underneath, a sector tag reading Housing & Construction, and a location of United States. A control reads View Quora profile. Under About: Caregiver right now. I have held many different jobs over the years. Jane of all trades master of none. Under Specializations, eight tags: Patient assessment and monitoring; Medication administration; Wound care and procedures; Patient education and triage; Structural design; Geotechnical assessment; Construction supervision; Site surveys and CAD. Below, a panel headed Want to work together? reads: the directory shows who is in the community and what they do. Want a service or good from this person? Look for them in Foundation, where members offer and exchange help — or browse Foundation to find someone else who can.]

On one side: patient assessment and monitoring, medication administration, wound care and procedures, patient education and triage.

On the other: structural design, geotechnical assessment, construction supervision, and site surveys and CAD.

Her own description is shorter than the list: caregiver right now, many different jobs over the years, a Jane of all trades and master of none. Her headline is two words — just about anything, and the profile files her under Housing & Construction, which is one of the two trades and not the other.

Why the range is the thing, not the caveat

Master of none is how people are taught to describe a working life that went in several directions. It assumes a room already full of specialists, where the only open position is the narrow one nobody has taken.

That is not the shape of this list. The app carries a catalog of 657 skills — the set a working economy of about five million people needs — and most of them have nobody in them at all. The list is empty rather than competitive.

An empty list is filled by people who can do more than one thing. Somebody who has held several jobs over several years has been paid to learn things that a person with one straight line has not had to. Being a caregiver right now and having done structural work before is two entire sectors covered by one person who already knows what both actually involve when the day goes wrong.

Medicine is the shortage this community feels hardest — SkillsHunt runs a standing mission to find a doctor, because a group this size needs one and does not have one. Nobody is claiming that patient assessment is being a doctor, and this post is not going to pretend otherwise. But triage, wound care and knowing when something needs somebody more qualified are what stand between a problem and a worse one, and they are on the list under her name.

How she came to be listed

Christy did not put herself in the Directory. I did, after reading what she said about herself in public, and the listing is marked community-generated so that nobody has to guess.

That is how most of the list was built, one person at a time. The Directory is the thing survivors ask for more than anything else: a findable list of people and what they can do. Building it by waiting for people to sign up for another app would have meant building nothing.

The invitation

Christy, both of those trades are on the list under your name, and neither has many people in it.

Claiming the listing is the step that makes it yours. It takes a free account and a few minutes, and after that the entry is under your control rather than mine — you set what it says, and you can take it down.

Nothing else is required. You do not have to use the rest of the app, you do not have to post anything, and you do not have to agree with anything I have written about why any of this is happening.

If the answer is no, that is a complete answer and nothing about your listing changes. You can have it taken down at any time, with no account involved anywhere in that. Nobody who has been asked has wanted theirs removed, but the offer has been real since the day the list went up.

This does not run one way

An invitation that only asks is not worth much to somebody already carrying a full week.

The list works in both directions. A caregiver needs a ride that does not run through somebody who should not be driving her. A person doing site work needs a room, a repair, a meal, an hour of a trade she does not have. Those are on the list too, under other people's names, and the same listing that makes her findable is what makes them findable to her.

That is the exchange, and it is deliberately the first thing here. Asking somebody for something real is also what filters out the people who will never hand you anything.

Where to find it in the app

- Directory (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#directory)
- SkillsHunt (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skills-hunt) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#skills-hunt)
- Foundation (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/foundation) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#foundation)
- Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#workforce)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

Full post: https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/an-invitation-to-christy

2026-09-17 · Follow This Blog Without an Account

There is now a feed for this blog, at https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/feed.xml.

If that means nothing to you, the next section is for you. If it does, that address is all you need.

What a feed is

A feed is a plain list of what has been published here — each post's title, its date, a few sentences saying what it is, and its address. It is written for software to read rather than for a person.

You point an app at it once. From then on the app checks the list for you and shows you what is new. You do not have to remember to come back, and nothing decides what you see first: posts arrive in the order they were written.

This is old technology and it is still the plainest arrangement anybody has come up with. It is also how every podcast in the world works. If you have ever subscribed to a podcast, you have used a feed and did not have to know it.

Why it is here

Quora has erased my accounts forty-seven times.

What those erasures took was never the writing. Every post is in this repository and I can rewrite anything. What they took was people knowing where I am. Each time an account went, everybody following it lost the thread, and most of them never found their way back — not because they stopped caring, but because there was no route from where they were to where I went.

A feed cannot be taken away like that. There is no account to suspend, no address I hold, and no company standing between you and the list. It is a file on the blog, the same blog that carries the posts. Anybody who has it stays connected to this whether or not any platform still lets me exist on it.

What it will not do

Most people do not use feed readers. They were common until Google closed the best one in 2013, and since then they have been a thing for people who already knew about them. If you have never installed one, you probably will not install one for a blog, and that is a reasonable decision.

It also does not reach you the way a message does. Nothing buzzes. You open your reader when you feel like it and the posts are sitting there, which some people much prefer and others will find useless.

So this is not the thing that solves being erased. It is one route that cannot be closed by anybody, sitting alongside the ones that can.

How to use it, if you want to

You need a reader app — there are many, most are free, and they run on a phone. Any of them will do; nothing about this feed is particular.

In most of them you can paste this blog's address on its own and the app will find the feed by itself. If yours asks for the feed address specifically, it is the one at the top of this post. There is also a "Follow by RSS" link in the footer of every page here.

The feed carries the newest fifty posts across everything published here — the posts, the product updates, the guides, the archive. It is rebuilt every time this blog publishes, so it is never behind. When I bring in old material from the erased accounts it is filed under the date it was originally written, which means it lands where it belongs in the record instead of arriving in your reader as fifty new things.

Why not an email list

Several people have asked, and the honest answer is that email is the thing most people use and also the thing most easily taken from me.

A mailing list lives or dies on whether the large mail providers think you are wanted. That judgment runs mostly on how many recipients mark a message as unwanted, and the threshold is low enough that a small number of people acting together can cross it for everybody. Once crossed, every subscriber stops getting mail, including the ones who asked for it. The service sending it removes the account and the list goes with it — which is the same shape as everything above, with a different company's name on it.

That is not a reason to never do it. It is a reason to build the thing that cannot be taken first, and to design the fragile one so that losing it costs me a send rather than the people.

Say something under this post

If you use a reader already, tell me which one — I would rather recommend something a person here actually uses than something I read about. If you tried to follow me somewhere and lost me when the account went, that is worth writing down too.

Reading the conversation under any post here takes no account at all.

Where to find it in the app

- Fireside (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/fireside) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#fireside)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

Full post: https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/follow-without-an-account
