2026-09-15 · Dictionary: Skills Economy Terms

This is a living page. It is kept current, and its date moves it to the top of the feed whenever it changes. Every change is on the public record in the repository's history.

Latest changes

2026-09-15 — TI Radio added to the capability table: a published schedule of live discussions members host in Chyme, at app.chargingthefuture.com/ti-radio. Reading it needs no account, which makes it the second page here that does not gate reading. Hosting needs an approved account; a member takes an empty 90-minute slot and writes what it is about. A listing is not an endorsement — a slot is taken, not granted, nobody reviews a description first, and the app's own card says so. The name is also the one exception to the rule below that TI is not part of a product name.

2026-09-13 — Fireside added to the capability table: threaded conversation under the posts on this blog. Anyone can read it without an account. Writing needs one, and what somebody writes becomes publicly visible once they are approved through Unlock.

2026-08-28 — LevelUp is now called SkillUp. Only the name changed: it is the same skills-training cohorts, where you earn credits as you reach each milestone. Its address changed with it, from /apps/level-up to /apps/skill-up, and the old address no longer opens — an old bookmark needs replacing.

2026-08-26 — The 384 entry gains its two meanings: the product benchmark on the way to an estimated five million, and the personal count of known-real people trading at once. The two-generation goal is added as the stretch goal. Both come from the post "The two-generation goal" (2026-08-26).

2026-08-24 — The ClickLog privacy line is corrected. It said notes are always private, which overstated it: notes are not shown to anyone else here and are not in the trend report. The same wording was corrected in four posts the same day.

2026-08-21 — Two lines added to the Specterati lexicon: "secret society" (the brag, and why the label is wrong by definition) and "collective amusement" (the engine behind the day-grading harassment).

2026-08-20 — Every capability in the table now links straight to it in the app, with its guide section beside it. Unlock added to the table — it was missing, and it is the door every member comes through. Every capability now has a guide section, so every row links to one. Mutual Time and Bug Reporting have no page of their own to open, so those two carry their guide section alone.

2026-08-18 — Full refresh. Skills Economy naming (the TSE abbreviation is retired). The member-count vocabulary added (signed up, recruited, Community Value Index, Value waiting to happen). ClickLog vocabulary added. The capability table rebuilt to match the app as it runs today; retired capabilities removed.

Platform and identity

Charging The Future — the organization and umbrella brand. Use the full name when referring to the company or mission.

Skills Economy (SE) — the product: the apps, community tools, and economic infrastructure built for survivors. Earlier writing says "TI Skills Economy" or "TSE"; those names are retired, and old posts keep them as historical record.

TI Radio — the one product name that keeps "TI" in it, and the only exception to the retirement above (owner decision, 2026-09-14). It is named for the space it was built to serve, and people arriving from there need to find the same name on both sides. Consistency for somebody crossing over is worth more here than the naming rule. Do not read it as a reopening: every other product name drops TI, and a second exception is the owner's decision rather than a precedent this one sets.

Survivors — the default word for the people here, in the app and in writing. Preferred over "victims" everywhere except legal or statutory references.

Targeted Individual (TI) — the term many survivors use for themselves and search by. Used as a bridge term; "survivors" is the default.

Psyop-Free Economy — the positioning phrase for the goal: economic participation outside Specterati influence.

Live, Work, and Prevail — the mission line.

Counting people (this is not a typical app)

Signed up / approved members — people who created their own account and were approved. The only number the 384 goal measures.

384 — the personal, realistic goal: 384 people working with each other at any given time. Not the far number; the one a person can watch arrive. The number carries two meanings. As a product benchmark, 384 people trading at once shows the app can grow toward the estimated five million survivors without breaking, and that it solves a real-world problem rather than a niche one — for this meaning, mixed real and fake participation still counts. As the personal goal, it counts only people personally known to be real, trading at any given time; the app does not verify anyone, so the verification here is personal knowledge.

Two-generation goal — the stretch goal past 384: what the Du Bois cohort accomplished in one generation, this community accomplishes in two (the ordinary kind, twenty-five to thirty years each) — its own economy, institutions, and public record, built under both extremes at once: outright trafficking, and outsized surveillance technology while outnumbered by headcount. The finish line is the third-generation Targeted Individual, who can exit from the moment their targeting starts. Stated in the post "The two-generation goal" (2026-08-26).

Recruited — people researched individually — skills stated in their own words, in public — and placed on the skills map with a community-generated directory profile. The capacity math runs on this number.

Community-generated profile — a directory profile created by a member on a survivor's behalf, labeled that way in the app and naming who made it. Claimable by its subject at any time, deletable on request. Nobody has asked; some were outraged at the suggestion, because the profile is how other survivors find them.

Community Value Index — value actually exchanged and settled in the community, cumulative since June 12, 2026. A relative index in the spirit of GDP. Never money, a price, or an exchange or redemption value.

Value waiting to happen (GDP Projected) — what the open posts on the board would add if every one of them closed. Most posts never close: it measures interest, not achievement. Not part of the Community Value Index, and not money.

The Specterati lexicon

Specterati — the replacement for the colloquial labels "gang stalking" and "gang stalker." Specter: a ghostly apparition, for the unseen and insidious nature of the harassment. -ati: a suffix for a group or collective, for a network working together.

Specterati operator — a person who carries out Specterati harassment. Used instead of "perp" in writing.

Specterati harassment — the organized harassment itself. Used instead of "gang stalking" in writing.

"Secret society" — the Specterati's own brag, and wrong by definition. A secret society keeps its membership and purpose hidden; the Specterati advertise themselves to their targets constantly — the signaling is the harassment. What they actually are is a control mechanism run by traffickers (sex, drugs, and human trafficking), with the harassment as its enforcement arm. The brag is recruitment theater, not a description.

Collective amusement — the engine behind much of the harassment, named plainly: schemes are run, and targets' days are scheduled and graded (see The Good Day, Bad Day in the schemes list), for the entertainment of the group watching. The audience is the point; the target's harm is the raw material.

Specterwave — a pervasive influence that spreads fear and control.

Specterforce — a powerful, unseen entity that enforces compliance.

Spectervox — specter plus vox (voice): the suppression of dissenting voices.

Specterbane — a destructive force that seeks to eliminate individuality and freedom.

Specterrealm — a domain ruled by fear and intimidation.

Specterrise — a rise in sinister activity and dominance in society.

Value and credits

ServiceCredits — the in-app credits unit members exchange with each other. A non-fiat internal unit: not money, not cash, not a currency, and never redeemable or withdrawable for fiat value. A credit movement is a send, a transfer, or an exchange — never a payment. Real fiat amounts elsewhere in the app (a LightHouse listing's actual rent, a confirmed donation) are real money and are described as such.

ClickLog vocabulary

Problems — the published list of 50+ things done to survivors, used to tag incidents. The full list is public on the landing page.

Schemes — the named, canonical list of Specterati methods, used to tag incidents. Grown over time from documented reports; new schemes earn a name through member suggestions.

Weavers of the Commons — the badge whose holders can suggest a new scheme while logging an incident, and who get a private Commons chat and a private Chyme audio room.

The three ClickLog privacy rules — notes are not shown to anyone else here and are not in the trend report; a private, untagged incident needs no location; tagging problems or schemes requires a location and trend sharing, because tags exist to feed the global map.

Other terms

Caught Red-Faced — a play on "caught red-handed": the guilt and embarrassment when a Specterati operator panics upon being caught committing a crime.

DEW — Directed Energy Weapon.

2026-09-15 · Peace Battle 2

A protest starts on Friday, September 18, 2026 at 7:00 PM Eastern.

It is called Peace Battle 2. The page with the countdown and everything you need to take part is here: chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/peace-battle-2 (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/peace-battle-2). The short form is /pb2 (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/pb2), which goes to the same place.

There is no address to travel to. That is the design, not a compromise.

Why it has a name

In 1903 W. E. B. Du Bois looked back at what the people who founded the colleges had done after the Civil War. Two thousand were trained. Those two thousand trained fifty thousand. Those fifty thousand taught nine millions. He called it the most wonderful peace-battle of the 19th century.

A battle fought with teaching and with work. And it was won.

Movements get names. The Civil Rights Movement had one. What is happening to Targeted Individuals has been going on without one, which makes it hard to point at and easy to wave off. So it gets a name, and the name says which lineage it belongs to.

The Skills Economy has been this since it started. It just did not have the label.

I am organizing it. I am not leading it. Survivors are a distributed group and nobody speaks for them, including me. Somebody has to call a date, so I am calling one.

A thing I said that was wrong

I used to say their methods were ingenious. I said it in conversation more than once.

That was callous, and it was also just inaccurate.

There is nothing clever about it. Look at the schemes one at a time and most of them are obvious to anyone standing nearby. People circle a person. Drama follows them from place to place. A stranger repeats something only a person's own household would know.

Somebody watching once can tell themselves there is another explanation. Somebody watching the same person for a year cannot. What they learn instead is that the plausible deniability is not really for them — it is for a courtroom. The behavior is arranged to sit in the gap where the law has nothing to charge.

That is not genius. It is criminals adapting faster than the legal framework, which is the same thing that happens everywhere else. Laws arrived late for social media. They arrived late for data privacy. They are arriving late here too, and human trafficking is harder than either, because the people running it keep changing shape while the statutes stay still.

Calling that ingenious hands them a compliment they did not earn. I am not saying it again.

And some people genuinely believe what they are told about a survivor. That is real and I am not pretending otherwise.

Why it is a protest, and why it is virtual

A protest is normally bodies in one place. Targeted Individuals have tried that. People have announced a protest and stood there by themselves.

It is not apathy. A protest in a place assumes three things: neighbors who are also affected, money to travel, and a day you can afford to lose. Most survivors have none of the three, because being made destitute is part of what is done to them.

And the spread is deliberate. Targeting does not concentrate in one neighborhood, one race, one class, or one religion. It is scattered across all of them, everywhere. That keeps the people it happens to from finding each other, and it lets anyone describe them as unrelated individuals with unrelated problems.

You can see it in what gets said about any one person. In my case the story changes with whoever is being recruited: terrorist for one audience, racist for another, prostitute for another, violent, impatient, hates Black men, hates Mexicans, hates dogs. The lie is chosen for the listener. It is not meant to be consistent. It is meant to divide.

So this protest is distributed too, because what it answers is distributed. It runs for years, which a protest in a physical place cannot do. It costs nothing but the minutes you give it. And it is the first form of this that a person who is broke, isolated, or too tired to leave the house can actually join.

Three ways to take part

Three, not ten. More than three and a protest turns into homework.

One: say something under a post

Read any post on this blog and leave a comment or a reaction under it. Reading the conversation needs no account at all. Writing takes a free one.

That is Fireside: app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/fireside (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/fireside).

This is the one that answers Quora directly. TrustTransport is the Uber for Targeted Individuals. Fireside is the Quora for Targeted Individuals.

More than twenty times an account of this project has been erased on Quora, and every comment under it went with it — mine and other people's. If you replied to me there, your words are gone and you did nothing wrong. The dated record is on old links, new links (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/old-links-new-links).

You are already writing for free on a platform that sells what you write and deletes it whenever it likes. Writing here costs you the same nothing and the words stay findable.

Two: take a slot on TI Radio

A published week of live audio rooms, at app.chargingthefuture.com/ti-radio (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/ti-radio). Take an open time, write a line about what it is about, and turn up.

Low pressure on purpose. Pick a time every week, sit in the room, and talk to whoever arrives. It does not have to be about being targeted. It can be about anything.

The aim is a schedule with somebody in it as often as possible. The rooms themselves run through Chyme (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/chyme). Listening needs no account; speaking does. A slot is taken, not granted, so a name on the schedule is not an endorsement from this project.

Three: post your 1%

Open What's your 1%? (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce?view=one-percent). It takes one percent of five million survivors — fifty thousand people — and runs the arithmetic backwards to you, weighted to your own trade.

Adjust the figures to what you would actually charge. Screenshot it. Put it where people will see it.

It costs a minute and it argues with nothing but arithmetic.

If you would rather act than post, do the other version: use the app to offer or ask for something real. Then the figure stops being a projection.

If you think none of this will work

Take part anyway, and here is the honest reason.

Nothing in it harms anybody. The Skills Economy does not ask you to fight, to confront, or to give anything up. It says: you were going to take that ride anyway, so check first whether another survivor can drive it.

Five million people leaving the global economy would not dent it. Not a little — not at all. And the people doing this already work to keep survivors from earning, which tells you they do not want that participation either. What they do not want is being named.

So the cost of being wrong about this is a minute of your time. The cost of being right and doing nothing is another generation.

What winning looks like

Peace Battle 2 is finished when a survivor can get 99% of what they need — housing, transportation, goods, services — from other survivors.

Not 100%. A phone and a vehicle need supply chains no community this size can run, and saying otherwise would be a lie. Nearly everything else can be grown, made, taught, driven, repaired, or arranged by somebody already on the list.

That is what exiting the psyop means in practice. Not leaving the global economy. Being able to come and go from it by choice, because what you need is available from people who are not part of what is being done to you.

The nearer marker is 384 approved members working with each other at any given time.

You can see the far end of it as a game: Peace-Battle 2 in the arcade (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/offline-os/apps/peace-battle-2/) starts from the 147 people on the Directory today and asks you to fill a catalog of 657 skills inside two generations. It is free, needs no account, and runs offline once it opens.

One generation is what I would like. Two is what I expect, given where we are starting.

Friday, September 18, 7:00 PM Eastern. chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/pb2 (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/pb2).

Where to find it in the app

- Fireside (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/fireside)
- TI Radio (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/ti-radio)
- Chyme (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/chyme) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#chyme)
- Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) — What's your 1%? (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce?view=one-percent) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#workforce)
- TrustTransport (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/trust-transport) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#trust-transport)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

2026-09-15 · TI Radio: a week of live discussions, and anyone can read it

Survivors host live discussions. Until now the only way to say one was happening was to announce it.

An announcement can point at one time. It cannot hold a schedule. And it cannot let somebody else put themselves on one.

So now there is a page that does both (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/ti-radio).

What it is

A week of live discussions, laid out in front of you.

The day is cut into 90-minute slots. Ninety minutes divides a day exactly sixteen times, so every day has the same sixteen starts. Nothing drifts. The grid runs seven days ahead.

Each row shows the time, who is hosting, and what it is about. The slot happening right now is marked "On air."

Every time is printed in your own timezone, and the page names the timezone it detected. You do not have to do the arithmetic.

An empty row is one somebody can take.

Reading it needs no account

This is the part worth saying plainly, because it is not how the rest of the app works.

You do not sign in. You do not get verified. You do not join anything. You open the page and read the schedule.

Almost every other screen here is behind approval, and for good reasons I have written about before. This one is not, and the reason is who it is for. The people it was built for have not joined anything yet. Telling them to sign up before they can even see when a discussion is happening asks for the thing at the end and gives nothing at the start.

So the schedule is open. Anyone can link to it. Anyone can plan around it.

Taking a slot

Hosting is different, and it does need an approved account.

An approved member presses an open row and writes what the discussion is about. They can add a few sentences for somebody deciding whether to come. The slot is then theirs, and their handle and subject appear on that row for everyone — including people who never sign in.

First come, first served. No queue, no application, nobody approving the topic.

At the time they booked, they and whoever turns up meet in Chyme.

Why hosting is gated when reading is not: putting your name on a public schedule is a commitment to turn up.

The limits, and why they are there

You can hold three slots in any 24 hours. Not three per calendar day — any 24-hour stretch. Three late one evening and a fourth after midnight is refused, the same as four in one afternoon. Otherwise one person could take a week.

You can give a slot back any time before it starts. It goes straight back to the guide as open.

You cannot give it back once it has begun. By then people have turned up.

An admin can remove any slot and is asked why. That is me, and it is one person.

What it is not

It is not Mutual Time. That is the other scheduling thing in the app, and it works the opposite way. An admin starts it. Everyone votes on when they are free. The app picks the hour with the most overlap. One meeting comes out of it, and only an admin can begin one.

TI Radio asks nothing. A member takes a time. A week of discussions comes out of it, side by side.

Both exist. They answer different questions.

It also does not own the room. The talking happens in Chyme, which keeps its own audio and its own records. TI Radio holds the schedule and nothing else. It never records who listened.

Why a schedule rather than an announcement

An announcement reaches whoever is looking the day it goes out.

A schedule sits there. Somebody who finds it on a Tuesday can see what is on Thursday. Somebody in another timezone can find the one slot that works for them. Somebody who is not ready to join anything can still turn up and listen.

And it is not mine to fill. I am not booking sixteen slots a day and calling it a station. The rows are empty until somebody takes one, and what appears there is whatever survivors decided to talk about.

Where to find it in the app

- TI Radio (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/ti-radio)
- Chyme (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/chyme) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#chyme)
- Mutual Time (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/mutual-time) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#mutual-time)
- Unlock (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/plugin/unlock) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#unlock)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

Full post: https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/ti-radio

2026-09-15 · What's Your Hangup?

If something about the Skills Economy stops you, I want to know what it is.

Not as a challenge. I am asking because I cannot answer an objection nobody says out loud, and because some of them are going to be right.

You can write it under this post. Reading takes nothing; writing takes a free account: app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/fireside (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/fireside).

Here is the one I hear most, and the one I want to answer properly.

"It is not a real job"

Underneath that is usually something like: I had a career, I was good at it, and this looks like a step down.

I understand it. I want to put the record next to it.

Destitution comes after the proof, not before

Look at when it happens.

A person gets educated. Gets hired. Gets good. Gets promoted. They are a productive participant in the global economy, by any measure that economy uses.

Then it starts. The references dry up. The role is restructured. The contract is not renewed. The next interview goes strangely. It compounds until there is nothing coming in.

The destitution arrives after the proof of capability, not instead of it. That order matters, and it is the same order for almost everybody this happens to.

If what they wanted was your work, they would have let you keep the job.

So your participation is not what they are after

This is the part worth sitting with.

Whatever is being extracted here, it is not your labor and it is not your output. They removed those on purpose, from people who were demonstrably good at producing them.

Five million people leaving the global economy would not dent it. Not a little — not at all. And they already push survivors out of it, which tells you they do not want that participation either.

This is the opposite of what was asked for before

Dr. King's last campaign was about money. Not symbolism, not seats — wages, jobs, and a union for sanitation workers in Memphis, and a Poor People's Campaign aimed at economic demands. The ask was to be let into the economy on fair terms.

He was killed in the middle of that work.

What happens to Targeted Individuals now is not that. There are deaths, and they are arranged to look like something else. But the public killing of somebody for making an economic demand is largely not the method anymore.

I can think of two reasons. One is that killing somebody in the open is no longer something a person can do and walk away from. The other is that what is wanted out of us changed — that the daily suffering of somebody living in destitution is now the thing being harvested, and a dead person stops producing it.

I do not know which. I am not sure it matters, because both land in the same place: they do not want you in that economy in any sense.

Their whole method runs on humiliation

Every scheme, when you strip it down, is built to make a person feel small in front of others. Shamed. Discredited. Not believed.

Knowing that changes the shape of the question.

If the method is humiliation, and the removal from work is part of the method, then staying loyal to an economy that already removed you is not principle. It is the last part of the humiliation still doing its job.

So what exactly is the hangup?

What working among them actually looks like from here

I want to be straight about the alternative rather than strawman it.

Until there is justice, there is no reliable path to working among them except the one they allow. And what gets allowed is narrow: roles where somebody else sets your hours, watches your day, and decides your pay.

That is not a judgment about the work or the people doing it. Every one of those jobs is real work and somebody has to do it. The point is which jobs stay available to a person who is being targeted, and why those particular ones do.

They are the ones with the most control in them.

The Skills Economy is built the other way round

You set the level of engagement. One ride a month, or nothing for six months, or every day.

Nobody schedules you. Nobody approves your rate. Nobody can remove you from it, because there is no gate for anybody to stand at — there is no second tier and no way to buy influence over another account.

And if you are ambitious, the ceiling is not set by whoever is willing to hire you. What's your 1%? (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce?view=one-percent) runs that arithmetic for your own trade: one percent of five million survivors is fifty thousand people, and it works backwards from there to what serving them would be worth in a year.

That figure is not a promise. It is what the arithmetic says is available to somebody who decides to go after it.

Back to the question

I would rather hear the objection than have you close the tab holding it.

If it is "I do not believe the numbers", say that. If it is "I do not trust an app", say that. If it is "I tried and nobody answered", say that one especially, because that is a thing I can fix.

Say it under this post. Nobody has to agree with me for this to be worth writing down.

Where to find it in the app

- Fireside (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/fireside)
- Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) — What's your 1%? (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce?view=one-percent) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#workforce)
- TrustTransport (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/trust-transport) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#trust-transport)
- Peace Battle 2 (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/peace-battle-2)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

Full post: https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/whats-your-hangup

==============================================================================
2026-09-15 · What's Your 1%?

There is a screen in the app called Workforce. It models what a population of five million people looks like as an economy: how many workers, which sectors, which jobs, how much output. It is where the $142,500-per-person figure comes from, and I explained all of it in How the math works (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/how-the-math-works).

It has one problem. It is entirely about everybody, and it never once speaks to the person reading it.

So people read the whole thing, follow every number, agree it adds up, and close it thinking: fine, but none of that is about me.

Today there is a new tab at the end of that screen. It is called What's your 1%?

What it does

It runs the same math backwards.

Workforce works forwards. Take a thriving economy's output, divide it evenly across everybody in it, and each person contributes about $142,500 a year. That number comes from Singapore, which publishes it, and it is the only place in the app where anything is stated in US dollars.

An even split is an average, and an average flattens exactly the thing worth seeing. Most people do not work full time. They have children, or illness, or a second thing they are doing, or they simply do not want to. The average has all of them in it. That is what makes it an average and not a ceiling.

So the new tab starts from the other end. One percent of five million is 50,000 people. The question is not what the average person contributes. It is: if you reached 50,000 people, what would that be?

| What one person pays you, once, in a year | Across 50,000 people | Compared to the average |
| $5 | $250,000 | 3.5× |
| $20 | $1,000,000 | 14× |
| $50 | $2,500,000 | 35× |
| $100 | $5,000,000 | 70× |
| $250 | $12,500,000 | 175× |

You do not have to believe a forecast. Find the row closest to what you already charge one person, once, and read across.

Five dollars. Not five dollars an hour, five dollars a year, from one person, once. That is a quarter of a million dollars. Three and a half times the average contributor in a country people hold up as a model economy. One percent puts an ordinary trade at the level the wider economy reserves for its top performers.

Why it is not the same screen for everybody

That table is a reference. As an answer it would be dishonest. Trades are not interchangeable. A screen that hands a plumber and a physiotherapist the same number tells neither of them anything.

So two things weight it to you.

The first the app works out on its own. It already models how many people doing your job a population of five million needs. That is what the Workforce screen has always been for. Turn that around and you get how many people one of you serves when your trade is staffed normally. For some trades that is a few hundred people. For others it is tens of thousands. Then it tells you how far 1% is beyond that: forty times your trade's normal load, or five times, depending on what you do. That gap is not hidden, because the gap is what the five routes are for.

I want to be exact about the limit on that, because it is real. The skills map carries a weight for each sector and not yet for each job title inside one. So it separates trades in different sectors properly, and two jobs inside the same sector currently land on the same figure. The screen says so where it shows the number. It will sharpen when the map carries a weight per job rather than per sector.

The second weight the app cannot work out, so it asks. How often does one person need you in a year? A hairdresser and a roofer do not have the same answer and no table in my app knows either of them. You type what you charge for one job and how often one person needs you, and it computes your own row. Less than once a year is fine — once every two years, once a decade, whatever your trade actually is.

Nothing you type is saved. It is not sent anywhere, it is not stored, and it is gone when you reload. It is a calculator, not a form.

I am not forecasting anybody's income and the screen says so plainly. These are speculative figures on a stated share of a population estimate. What actually happens takes talent and work and luck in some combination, and no screen hands you those.

The part people get stuck on

Nobody reaches 50,000 people one job at a time.

That is true, and it is the reason 1% sounds absurd the moment you hear it. A plumber can only be in one house. A hairdresser can only cut one head of hair. A mechanic has two hands.

It is a ceiling on one route. It is not a ceiling on the skill.

So the tab lists five of them, and each one links to the part of the app that carries it.

Do the work itself, in person or by traveling to it. One person at a time, and limited by where you can physically be. That is Foundation.

Take the same question by call instead. A plumber diagnoses a problem down a phone line every day of the week and charges for it. Anyone, anywhere, no travel for either of you. Foundation again.

Work through a problem live with a group watching. Everybody in the session at once, rather than one person per hour. That is PeerProgramming.

Take apprentices. Teach the trade to people starting from nothing. You reach everybody you train, and then everybody they go on to serve. That is SkillUp.

Write down what you know, once. Everybody who asks that question afterwards reads it, including people you will never meet. That is the Knowledge Library.

The list is fixed. It does not get cleverly generated for your particular trade, because a generated list would invent routes that sound plausible and do not exist. These five exist.

Why I built it

Two beliefs, and both are wrong.

The first is that the Skills Economy cannot work. I have answered that one already, at length, with numbers anybody can check — If you say it cannot be done, present the math (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/present-the-math).

The second one is quieter and it does more damage. It is a person deciding they no longer have skills or value.

That belief does not arrive out of nowhere. It arrives because a great deal was taken, and because the taking was designed to look like a verdict.

What was actually taken

Organized crime may have taken a lot from you. The career. The credentials. The clients. The income, the standing, the ordinary assumption that a résumé leads somewhere.

Here is what does not follow from that.

What was taken was taken inside a system that runs on trafficking. It was never a neutral market that assessed you and found you wanting. It is an arrangement in which people are moved into place, held in place, and worked. Being pushed out of it is not a measurement of your worth. It is a description of who runs it.

The skill did not go anywhere. A plumber who was driven out of work is still a plumber. A nurse who was hounded out of a hospital still knows what they know. That knowledge does not live in an employer's file, and nobody can repossess it. The thing you lost was access to a market. That is not the same as losing the ability.

And you can rebuild. Not get it back — rebuild, which is a different and slower thing, and which is what the routes above are for.

The ceiling is not a modest one

I have used a plumber throughout because trades make the math legible. It is not the limit.

You could be a social media influencer. An athlete. A tech founder. You could reach the same scale of success as the accepted members of the psyop. Their position came with the arrangement. They are where they are because they went along with it.

The difference is that you would do it without grifting.

Their version required participating in trafficking. It required somebody to be worked, and somebody to be kept in place, and a person willing not to look at either. That is the entry fee, and it is why so many of the people at that level are so obviously hollow up close.

Yours does not require that. Reaching 50,000 people by being good at something, and being paid by them, has no one in it who had to be harmed. The scale is the same. The thing underneath it is not.

So when the arithmetic on that screen looks large, that is not the app flattering you. That is what reaching a lot of people is worth when nobody had to be trafficked for you to reach them.

About mourning the life you had

You are allowed to grieve it. It was real, you built it, and it was taken.

I am not going to tell anybody how long that should take, or what it ought to look like. I have no opinion about how somebody carries it. Grief is not an obstacle to work around and it is not a character flaw. It is the correct response to a loss.

What I will say is that the grief and the rebuilding are not in competition. Nobody has to be finished with the first before starting the second. Nobody has to feel good about anything to list a skill.

An agent of anything advocates for it

Here is a thing worth having in your head the next time somebody tells you what you deserve.

A car salesperson believes you deserve the car. They will tell you so, with feeling, and they are not lying exactly. They work for the dealership. The car is what they have to offer, and wanting you to have it is the job. A grocery chain believes everybody deserves groceries. Their groceries. A recruiter believes you deserve a better role, which is one on their books.

Everyone selling something argues that you deserve the thing they are selling. That is not a moral position. It is a sales position, and once you see the shape of it you see it everywhere.

Now apply it.

When somebody tells a survivor they deserve what is being done to them, that is the same move. Not an assessment. Advocacy for their own operation. They are agents of trafficking, so trafficking is the product they push. They push it the way every agent does: by telling you that you deserve it.

One of them wrote to me. A person who calls themselves a happiness coach, Rebecca L. Norrington, emailed me to say they enjoy this.

A happiness coach. That is the self-description, published, alongside an email saying they enjoy watching a person be targeted. A coach in that trade is meant to have exactly one product. They were selling the other thing while wearing the label of the first.

So: nothing they told you about your worth is information about you. It is a pitch. You can decline it the same way you decline any other pitch, which is by noticing who benefits.

What the card shows

The tab opens with a card, and the card is you. Your initials, your name, your trade, and the skills from your Directory listing laid out like a playing card.

It is yours only. Nobody else sees it. You cannot look anybody else up. There is no score, no rank, and nobody to be above or below. If you have not claimed your Directory listing yet, the card says so and asks you to. It will not invent a name and a trade for you.

Underneath it is what your trade normally carries, then the two boxes for your own numbers, then the reference table, then the five routes.

There is nothing else on it. It does not ask you to do anything and it does not applaud you. It does the arithmetic and leaves it on the screen.

The skill you have is still a skill, and the number it could reach here is not small.

2026-09-15 · You Can Talk Under These Posts Now

There is now a conversation under every post on this blog. It is called Fireside, and it sits at the bottom of every one of them.

Reading it takes nothing

No account. No sign-in. No box asking you to create something before you are allowed to see what people said.

That is deliberate, and it is backwards from where most of you read me. Quora will not show you a comment thread without a login. Fireside does the reverse: anybody who can read the post can read the conversation under it, whether they have ever heard of this app or not.

Writing takes an account, and here is exactly what happens

You sign in, you write, and your comment saves. It is not publicly visible yet. It becomes visible when you are approved, and the screen tells you that at the moment you press post — not afterwards, and not by leaving you to work out why nothing appeared.

Approval is per person, not per comment. When somebody is approved, everything they have written here becomes visible at once, including what they wrote before. So the waiting is not wasted and you are not writing into a hole.

What approval involves is one thing: a Quora profile link. A person reads it. That is the same look this app does before anyone is approved, and writing a comment is a reasonable place to start it, because judging what somebody wrote is the same judgment.

Three reactions rather than an open set of emoji — I recognize this, This helped, Same here — so a count means the same thing on every comment. Replies go one level deep and stop there, because anything deeper is unreadable on a phone, and a phone is the only width this app has.

Why this exists

Quora has erased more than twenty of my accounts. Each time, everything posted from that account went with it, and so did every comment under it — mine and everybody else's. Conversations I had with people over two years are gone from where they happened. If you replied to me there, your words are gone too, and you did nothing wrong.

Two other things are true about comments on that platform, and they are quieter problems. You cannot search them. You cannot bookmark one. A good answer somebody wrote three months ago is functionally lost even while the account is alive.

A comment under this post has an address. It is stored on a system this project runs, it survives any platform deciding anything, and it can be found again.

What you control, and what nobody else does

Take your own comment down at any time, with no admin involved and no reason required. The words go. The row stays only so a reply underneath it does not lose its place, and it is shown to nobody.

Fireside carries the same deletion rights as the group chat, from the same account screen. If you delete your account, your comments and reactions go with it.

There is one exception and it is worth reading twice. A comment can be copied into this blog's own published pages — searchable, and captured by web archives. That takes two separate yeses: you ask for it, and an admin agrees. It is off unless you turn it on, nobody else can turn it on for you, and you can turn it back off right up until the copy is made. The reason it needs both is that a web capture cannot be recalled afterwards by anybody, this project included, and a page that is permanent should not be one person's mistake or one account's bad faith.

Nothing is under any post yet

This went up today, so every thread on the blog is empty. Somebody has to be first, and it may as well be about whichever post you actually have something to say about.

There are few rules here on purpose. The group chat's guidelines apply, and beyond them not much, because a conversation about what was done to people and what to do next does not survive being tidied into a format.

Where to find it in the app

- Fireside (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/fireside)
- Unlock (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/plugin/unlock) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#unlock)
- Commons (https://app.chargingthefuture.com) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#commons)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

Full post: https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/you-can-talk-under-these-posts-now

2026-09-14 · Nobody Calls to Tell You Your Project Is Dead

On Thursday, September 10, I had a conversation with somebody on the app who I had already concluded was a Specterati operator. I had been keeping our contact short for a while. They kept asking for phone calls that turned out to be about nothing, and after enough of those I stopped treating the requests as ordinary.

They asked for another call. On it, they told me the app is dead. Nobody is on it. They are getting no value from it.

Then, in the same conversation, they asked me to ask them to stay.

Then they asked for advanced permissions, so they could help bring more people in. They have not brought one.

Three tells in one call

Take the first on its own. A person who opens an app, finds nothing they want, and gets nothing out of it does not schedule a call to say so. They stop opening it. Nobody in the history of software has picked up the phone to inform a developer that their project has no users. That sentence is not a review. It is something you say to a person you want to stop.

The second one is stranger than the first and gives it away. If the place is empty and worthless, there is nothing to be asked to stay for. Wanting to be invited to remain somewhere you have just called dead is not a position anybody holds. It is a move, and the move needs the first sentence to set it up.

The third says what the first two were for. Admin access, to go and get users — from somebody whose contribution to date is zero invitations. The sequence is the app is failing, I am still here, let me in deeper so I can fix it. Each part exists to make the next one sound reasonable.

I want to be exact about what I am claiming. I am not saying anybody who criticizes the app is an operator, and I am not saying this because the criticism stung. Criticism is useful and I get plenty of it. What I am describing is a shape: an unprompted declaration that the project is finished, delivered by voice, to the one person whose continuing is the thing at stake, followed immediately by two asks that only make sense if the declaration is believed.

There are no advanced permissions to give

The request was not going to be granted, and not because I had made up my mind about the person. There is nothing there to grant.

The app has one thing a member can do with another member: provide material value. Somebody needs a ride, a repair, a place to stay, an hour of a trade they do not have. Somebody else has it. They arrange it between themselves, both sides come out of it with something real, and then it is finished. The exchange settles and there is nothing left over.

That structure is deliberate, and it is the part that frustrates a person who came to do something else.

There is no staff to get to. On a platform of any size, an operator's most effective route is not their own account — it is somebody on the inside who can restrict an account, remove a post, or lose a report. Several of my own accounts elsewhere have gone that way. Here the moderation surface is small on purpose, and it is one person.

There are no ads to buy and no feed to place anything in. There is nothing to pay for at all.

You cannot follow a person around. There is no follower graph to build, no way to attach yourself to somebody's activity and be present wherever they are.

Swapping between identities does not work either. Nobody is required to use a legal name, but moving between accounts in a way meant to deceive gets caught, and account approval is a person looking at a real account with a public history behind it.

So what is left for somebody whose purpose is disruption is to provide material value like everybody else, which is the one thing they will not do. That is why the conversations kept wanting to be phone calls. The call is the only place left where the structure does not apply.

About the app being empty

The version of this that is worth answering is the one where a real Targeted Individual passes through, sees little happening, and leaves thinking the same thing.

So let me put the numbers where the app's own screens put them, and then say what they mean.

People sign up and are approved one at a time. The skills map is built differently — I research people individually, from skills they have stated publicly in their own words, and add them to the Directory. Being listed and having an account are separate things, and most of the list was built for people rather than by them. Three survivors have helped me directly this year. None of that passed through the app, and all three are on the Directory. The listing is what made them findable; the help happened between people.

Now the part about pace.

Traffickers run large communities and they run them on grift. The population is achieved by theft, by coercion, and by violence — people are moved into place, people are kept in place, and people who want to leave find out what leaving costs. When an operation like that needs a crowd to appear somewhere, a crowd appears, because the people in it were not asked.

I am building something else: a way for people to live outside the psyop and keep living. A thing built without stealing anybody, without extorting anybody, and without coercing anybody does not fill on command, because there is no command. There is nobody I can order to show up on a Tuesday to make a screenshot look better. There never will be. Everyone here shows up when they want to and when they can, and that is the arrangement working rather than the arrangement failing.

There is also the ordinary part, which I will not dress up. This is a product with a lot of features, in its third version, serving a group that is marginalized specifically by organized crime. For an ordinary app at this stage, the activity would be unremarkable. For this one, add that a serious amount of effort goes into keeping the people it serves isolated from each other. Both things are true and neither is a surprise.

Two years ago, telling survivors that a community of their own could exist got two answers. That it was a pipe dream, or that it was dangerous — that gathering would make everybody's targeting worse. Some of the people who said that have since changed their minds. That change took two years and it happened without anybody being stolen.

What I did about the account

I stopped the calls. Not by announcing it, and not by accusing anybody of anything, just by declining. What is left is writing, in the Commons group chat, where it is in public and on the record and I can answer at a time I choose. When something is out of line I say so plainly, and the behavior adjusts for a while.

The account stays. I do not delete accounts on suspicion, and I want to be clear that this is a position rather than a technicality. Being removed from a place on somebody's suspicion, with no process and no appeal, is done to survivors constantly — it is a large part of why I am building this at all. I am not going to import it here on day one because I am confident about a read. I am confident about this read. Confidence is not the standard.

The standard is conduct against the rules, which are short and which apply the same way to everybody. If this person crosses them, the account goes for what they did, and that will be legible to anybody who looks.

There is one more reason to leave it be, and it is practical. Organized crime does not give up when a route closes; it finds another one. Watching what somebody tries next, in a place where trying it does not work, is how the next mitigation gets built before it is needed. We have always had to adapt faster than the people working against us. That has not changed and it is not going to.

Where to find it in the app

- Unlock (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/plugin/unlock) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#unlock)

2026-09-14 · What I Missed About Wanting Everything On the Blockchain

Anybody who spent time around crypto before about 2022 has met the person who wants everything on a blockchain. Not just money — the whole application. Put the rental marketplace on chain. Put the ride sharing on chain. Put all of it on chain.

My position then was that this was tunnel vision. Crypto is useful for the financial part of a transaction. It is not necessary to run the software around the transaction, and bolting a chain onto a booking system makes the booking system worse. Somebody would say "put a rental marketplace on the blockchain" and I would think: you do not need that, you need a payment you can make without asking permission.

I have not been active in that space for a few years. In the narrow technical sense I still hold most of that position.

What I got wrong was the motive.

What changed

I have been targeted since I was five years old. For most of that time it was covert — I could see it, but they did not want me to be certain. On January 1, 2023 it went overt, which is a deliberate stage: they want you to know it is happening, and it runs continuously.

Correction, 2026-09-18: this originally gave the date as January 1, 2024. The year was wrong; the overt phase restarted on January 1, 2023.

Living inside that changes what certain sentences sound like.

"I want this on a blockchain" is one of them. Read it again from inside a system where people can reach into your accounts, your records, your access, and your standing, and adjust them at will. It stops sounding like enthusiasm for a technology. It starts sounding like somebody saying: I want a thing that nobody can reach into.

I am not claiming that everybody who wants this is a survivor of organized crime. Plenty are engineers who find the technology interesting, and some are the people doing the harassing. Both are true. What I am saying is that there is a third reading I did not have access to before, and I suspect it is more common than the conversation lets on. The person is not asking for a database. They are asking to be out of reach.

Why I am thinking about it now

On Thursday, September 10, I had a conversation with somebody on the app who is a Specterati operator. I wrote separately about what they said about the community, in Nobody Calls to Tell You Your Project Is Dead (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/nobody-calls-to-say-its-dead), and I will not repeat it here.

The part that belongs in this post is what they asked for: advanced permissions in the app.

They wanted a lever. Somewhere in the system there would be a control that lets one person affect another person's experience — the ability to promote, restrict, approve, feature, moderate, or remove. That is the thing worth having if your purpose is to reach people. It is also the thing every platform hands to somebody.

There is no such control here, and the request is how I know what they came for. Anybody who has used the app for a week knows there is nothing to ask for, because everything is already available to them.

What it actually means to have no lever

The Skills Economy is a peer-to-peer network. Two people arrange something between themselves, on their own terms, and the app's job is logistics — helping them find each other and keep track — not sitting in the middle of the exchange as a referee.

That works because of who it is for. It is for adults, all of whom are targeted, and every one of us is somewhere different in that experience. The schemes vary, and so does the technology used against any given person. Directed Energy Weapons are part of it for me now and were not when I was young; for somebody else the timeline is completely different. From outside the operation you cannot know exactly why, though it appears that the higher somebody is on the list, the more severe it gets. A person in that situation does not need a moderator deciding what they are ready for. They need to be able to act on their own read of their own life.

So, concretely:

There is no permission ladder. When somebody is approved, they have everything. There is no second tier to reach, nothing to be granted later, and no way to pay me for more.

You cannot buy access and you cannot buy influence over somebody else's account. Not for money, not for credits, not for being helpful to me personally.

The audio room is open. Anybody can listen without an account; signing in is what lets you speak. That much transparency early costs an operator a great deal, because fraudulent behavior is visible from the first minute rather than after somebody has been harmed.

Going further than the open room requires the other person to agree. A one-to-one conversation cannot be started cold — it is an invitation that has to be accepted, it only exists while both people are already in the room, and declining sends the person who asked nothing at all. Paid work runs through the Foundation board, in the open, rather than through a private channel somebody talked their way into.

What an operator is after is harm — a person's degradation is the material they extract value from. None of the moves that produce it survive this structure for long, and what does get through is small.

What exiting actually costs, said honestly

I am not going to pretend the exit is complete, because it is not, and a person deciding what to do with their life deserves the real number.

Some people cannot leave the wider economy even if they want to. If you have a partner, children, or family depending on you, exiting means all of them exiting, and the Skills Economy cannot meet every one of their needs yet. That is a limit of what exists so far, not a failure of nerve on their part.

Some people do not want to, which is a complete answer and needs no defense.

And some things are simply not made here. The Skills Economy does not manufacture phones. If you want the current hardware and the current goods, you are going to obtain them through the ordinary economy, which means through people who are part of what is being done to us — and the device itself is compromised either way. Keeping that is keeping one foot in. It can be a reasonable trade. It should be a knowing one.

Back to the blockchain

None of this runs on a chain, and it does not need to.

When two people arrange an exchange here, the exchange happens between them. A driver and a rider settle it however they both agree — crypto, ordinary money, a straight barter, or free, which is a real and common option rather than a rounding error. The app records that something was exchanged. It does not sit in the middle of it, take a cut, or hold anybody's funds.

That is the thing the maximalists were reaching for, in my reading of them now. Not a specific data structure. A system with no seat in the middle for somebody to occupy.

You can get there by putting everything on a chain. You can also get there by building software that never creates the seat in the first place, and by refusing to add one when somebody asks you for it — which somebody will, and recently did.

- Chyme (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/chyme) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#chyme)
- Foundation (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/foundation) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#foundation)

Her profile is still up and the Directory entry still carries what she can do. Every platform hands its controls to somebody else, which makes it somewhere a Specterati operator (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/chargingthefuture/Dictionary) can work — so the list is kept somewhere no platform reaches.

The invitation

Janie (https://www.quora.com/profile/Janie-Spears-7), the Cleaners / Janitorial cohort has no trainer. You manage cleaners. You would be teaching people the thing you already do.

The terms are in the last post (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/rfs-trainers) and they are short. You set the schedule and the cadence. It runs remotely, because the people enrolling could be anywhere. You do not need to be a professional instructor — you need to know the work well enough that somebody who does not can follow you.

One step comes first and it is short: claim the listing. Cleaning is on it now, and the app matches a trainer to a cohort by the skills on a profile somebody has claimed — so claiming yours is what makes it count.

If the answer is no, that is a complete answer and nothing about your listing changes. You can also have the listing taken down at any time, which has been true since the day it went up.

This does not run one way

A trainer is not a donor.

People who can teach need things too, and the list is how they find them. Somebody who can run a cohort on cleaning may still need a repair, a ride, or an hour of somebody else's trade — and mechanics are one of the thinner parts of the map right now.

That is the shape of the thing rather than a slogan. The reason to be findable is that everybody is short of something, and the people short of different things are the ones who can settle it between them.

2026-09-13 · Request For Skills: trainers

LevelUp is SkillUp now. Same thing, clearer name.

The gap

Three cohorts are open in SkillUp (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skill-up). Every one of them reads Trainer TBD.

- Journalists / Reporters
- Construction Laborers
- Cleaners / Janitorial

[Screenshot — The Browse Cohorts screen in SkillUp, headed Browse Cohorts — enroll in a training program and grow your skills. Three summary cards read Open Cohorts 3, My Cohorts 0, and In Escrow 0 SC. Below a search box, three cohort cards, each marked Open. Journalists / Reporters: Trainer TBD, 3 milestones. Trainer earns 30 SC per learner, 30 SC from 1 enrolled. You get back 50 SC, your deposit, as you finish each milestone. Seats 11 left. Returnable deposit 50 SC. Enroll. Construction Laborers carries the same figures: Trainer TBD, 3 milestones, trainer earns 30 SC per learner, 30 SC from 1 enrolled, you get back 50 SC as you finish each milestone, seats 11 left, returnable deposit 50 SC, Enroll. Cleaners / Janitorial carries them again: Trainer TBD, 3 milestones, trainer earns 30 SC per learner, 30 SC from 1 enrolled, you get back 50 SC as you finish each milestone, seats 11 left, returnable deposit 50 SC, Enroll.]

Seats are filling. Enrolling holds a deposit of 50 credits, and you get every one of them back — they are returned to you as your trainer signs off each milestone, and if you leave the cohort the whole remaining hold comes back at once. So the course costs nothing in the end. What it asks for is 50 credits you can put down while you are in it.

I did not pick those three. SkillUp generates its own cohort recommendations from the distance between what the skills map holds and what people are looking for, and those came back as the widest gaps. People are enrolling against them now, and nobody is scheduled to teach any of them.

[Screenshot — The Trainers tab in SkillUp, headed Trainers — survivor-advocates who lead the cohorts. The list is empty. An icon of two people sits in the middle of the screen above the words No trainers listed yet, and below that: Trainers are survivor-advocates who lead cohorts. New trainers appear here as they join.]

So this is a Request For Skills, trainer edition.

What running one involves

You set the schedule and the cadence. Not me, and not the app.

Remote by default, because the cohorts are open globally — somebody who enrolls is as likely to be five time zones away as five miles. Run it inside the app or on whatever video tool you already use. The app does not care which one, and I am not going to recommend one.

In person is acceptable on one condition: it stays open to anybody who enrolled and cannot be there in person. A call running alongside it, a recording, a second sitting — whatever it takes. A cohort does not become a local one because the trainer is local.

You do not need to be a professional instructor. You need to know the work well enough that somebody who does not can follow you.

Two things the app checks rather than anybody approving you. Your own Directory listing has to carry a skill belonging to the occupation the cohort trains, and the listing has to be one you have claimed. That is the entire gate — a match between what you say you can do and what the cohort teaches, with nobody sitting in judgment of it.

Training earns credits. A trainer receives 10 credits per learner each time they sign off a milestone, up to double that where the skills map shows the widest gap. Those credits are minted for the work. They are not taken out of what the learner put down, which comes back to the learner.

Or nominate a trainer you know

You do not have to be the one teaching. If you know somebody who could run one of these, nominate them in SkillsHunt (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skills-hunt) — a name, where they are, what they can do. If the nomination is accepted, you receive credits for it.

They do not have to be a survivor, and they do not have to have an account. Most of the skills map was built by people naming somebody else.

If the software is what stops you

If you would run a cohort but the tooling would not carry what you want to do, say so in the Commons group chat (https://app.chargingthefuture.com) and I will build it.

Describe what would let you train more people, or train them better, and it gets built.

Where to find it in the app

- SkillUp (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skill-up) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#skill-up)
- ServiceCredits (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/service-credits) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#service-credits)
- Directory (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#directory)
- SkillsHunt (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/skills-hunt) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#skills-hunt)
- Commons (https://app.chargingthefuture.com) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#commons)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

Full post: https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/rfs-trainers

2026-08-29 · If you say it cannot be done, present the math

There is a thin line between offering an opinion and manipulating somebody. It is thinner than people think, and the thing that decides which side you are on is whether there is anything underneath what you said.

An unsupported claim, stated with confidence, to people who are looking for direction, does the same work as a lie. It does not matter whether the person meant it as one.

The claim

An economy run by Targeted Individuals (TIs), with no reliance on the Specterati, cannot be built.

I have read that on Quora many times. I have never once seen anybody show it.

Mine first

The first simulation (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/offline-os/apps/peace-battle/) replays something that already happened, and the figures in it are not mine.

W. E. B. Du Bois, "The Talented Tenth", in The Negro Problem, 1903. Public domain at https://www.gutenberg.org/ebooks/15041. He is describing what the college founders had already built after the war:

they founded colleges, and up from the colleges shot normal schools, and out from the normal schools went teachers, and around the normal teachers clustered other teachers to teach the public schools; the college trained in Greek and Latin and mathematics, 2,000 men; and these men trained full 50,000 others in morals and manners, and they in turn taught thrift and the alphabet to nine millions of men, who to-day hold $300,000,000 of property.

2,000 to 50,000 is 25 times over. 50,000 to 9,000,000 is 180 times over. In the United States alone.

The simulation gives you those two rates and one decision each round: how much goes to teaching, how much goes to the work itself. Put it all into the work and you reach 360,000 people. Teach first and you reach the 9,000,000 he recorded. Same effort, same number of rounds.

The people Du Bois was writing about were trafficked in public. It was overt, and it was openly not treated as a human rights violation. A modern TI is trafficked just as overtly, and it is just as unrecognized. The two situations are not the same and I am not saying they are — the routes differ, the spectrums differ. What matches is the operating condition: it happens in the open and no institution names it. That is the condition those 2,000 people built under. They did not wait to be believed first.

Outnumbered at least three hundred to one

In a town I had just arrived in, I counted the people involved on foot within 24 hours. I stopped at 300.

Not 300 over years. Not 300 who know me. Three hundred inside a single day, in a place I had never been, already there when I got there. That is not a hometown holding a grudge. Coverage like that is administered, and it is not administered locally.

300 is where I stopped counting. It is not where it ends.

None of that changes a number above. The rates describe what teaching does; they say nothing about how many people are working against you. Compounding is not a contest — 25 times over is 25 times over whether one person opposes it or a thousand do.

Thirty years is the conservative reading of that, which is why I hold a two-generation goal (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/the-two-generation-goal) rather than a shorter one.

Where this actually stands

The obvious answer is that I do not have 2,000 people. That is correct, and the number is not a secret.

The simulation has a second preset for it. On August 28 it read 101 people recruited, covering 160 of 657 skills. Those figures come off the Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) screen, which is where the skills map lives — open it and count.

101 against 2,000 is about a twentieth. It changes where the arithmetic starts and not what the arithmetic does, because a rate is a rate regardless of the number in front of it. That preset is in the simulation for the same reason the source is open: the weakest version of my own case should be the one you can see.

Now yours

If you say it cannot be done, present the math.

Not an opinion about whether people will participate. Not a prediction about human nature. Not a story about somebody who let you down. The numbers: a starting count, a rate at which capability passes from one person to the next, and the arithmetic that lands lower than the figures above.

Both simulations are free, run offline once opened, need no account, and send nothing anywhere.

The source is open. The first one (https://github.com/chargingthefuture/offline-os/tree/main/apps/peace-battle) and the second (https://github.com/chargingthefuture/offline-os/tree/main/apps/peace-battle-2) are both readable in full, and Du Bois's book is committed beside them. The rates are not something you have to take from me. They are two numbers in a file, and every figure I have quoted comes out of them. Check the arithmetic before you argue with the conclusion, and if the arithmetic is wrong, say where.

Change the inputs and show me what you get.

What the question sorts

Some of the accounts repeating it are there to repeat it. Spreading the belief that nothing can be built is a cheap way to keep people apart, and it costs nothing to post.

I cannot prove that about any particular account and I am not going to try. The question does that work instead. Somebody who is there to spread it has nothing to bring, because there was never any arithmetic behind it — that was the point of saying it.

And if you are not one of them, and you believe it, and you go looking for what your version rests on and find nothing there, that is worth knowing.

I am not shaming anybody for being isolated

Isolation is a reasonable response to what has been done to people. Pulling back after being set up, followed, lied about and cut out of work is not a defect, and I am not asking anybody to feel bad about it.

What does not hold together is complaining about having nothing while arguing against the thing that would produce something. Those are two positions and they cancel each other.

Here is how somebody holds both without noticing. Pain that comes from outside gets weighted heavily. Pain we cause ourselves gets discounted — the same act, the same cost, and it registers as smaller because we were the one who did it. We forgive ourselves faster than we forgive anybody else, and much faster than we forgive somebody who wronged us.

For a Targeted Individual that gap is wider, and it is wider on purpose. The apparatus is built to hold your attention on them. Every incident is an invitation to spend another day accounting for what they did.

So the outrage points outward at full volume, and the cost of staying apart barely registers, because that cost is self-inflicted and self-inflicted is the kind we discount.

Constantly obsessing over them is the reverse of freedom. It is the outcome they are working toward, reached without them having to do anything further.

The targeting is done to you. That was never a choice and it is not what I am talking about. But "it cannot be done" is a position, and a position can be checked. If it turns out there were never any numbers under yours, then what is keeping you apart from other survivors at that point is the position, not them.

The second simulation

The second one (https://chargingthefuture.github.io/offline-os/apps/peace-battle-2/) is about the part Du Bois did not have to solve.

His people were denied education. The deficit was skills, and the answer was to build them. Skills Economy starts from the opposite end: the people here are already highly skilled, and what is missing is discovery. Somebody with twenty years in a trade is not short of the trade. They are short of anybody knowing they have it.

So it is a map, and what spreads across it is isolation — a place where somebody needs something and nobody findable can do it. Four people with skills move against it. Clearing a place puts it in the Directory, and it stays covered from then on.

The Directory is a running list of Targeted Individuals alongside what they can do, one person at a time. It is the thing survivors ask for most. Being on it costs nobody an account: most entries were built for people rather than by them, and anybody can claim theirs or have it taken down. Seeing it does take a sign-in. It was public in version 2, and everything in it came from public sources — but gathered and sorted this way it becomes something else, so version 3 put it behind a login.

Where to find it in the app

- Directory (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/directory) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#directory)
- Workforce (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/apps/workforce) — guide (https://app.chargingthefuture.com/guide#workforce)

To sign up: https://chargingthefuture.com. It is free, invite-only, and you can use one part of it and ignore the rest.

Full post: https://chargingthefuture.github.io/chargingthefuture/article/wiki-site/present-the-math
